Character Options 


 
CASTLE ARMORY SERVICE WEAPON CATALOG 
 
This is a comprehensive catalog of all of the weapon categories CASTLE armory gives to its exorcists. Exorcists can use these tags to modify the 
effects of a weapon to better suit their style or preferences. It aims to make combat with mundane weapons a little less stale, heavily WIP. NOT 
recommended unless you really feel weapons are too boring. Every exorcist has two individual service weapons. 
 
Weapon Tags: 
Weapon tags follow the following rules: 
●​
All weapon tags must result in a net positive or net neutral point total. You cannot add tags that go into the negative on points. 
●​
Starting service weapons have a two tag maximum and 1 point to spend each (melee and ranged).  
○​
Tag maximum increases to 3 at character CAT2, 4 at CAT3, and 5 at CAT4. 
○​
You may spend 4 scrip to add 1 bonus point to spend, maximum of 3 bonus points. 
●​
Service weapons can be “re-specced” and have their tags changed during downtime for no cost by simply picking another weapon. 
○​
If you choose to use the Heretic Black Market weapon fuses, they can be transferred for no cost to the new weapon. 
●​
Mementos from Diligence’s high blasphemy Auteur cannot be tagged. If a tagged weapon is turned into a Memento, it loses its tags. 
●​
While beneficial, tags aren’t purely positive or negative. For example, if you’re attacking a thick armored shell with a Battering or Slash 
weapon, you aren’t going to be very effective, but attacking with a Heavy weapon may help reduce kickback or being thrown around. 
Additionally, if something would fall under the purview of a tag you don’t have you will similarly be at a disadvantage, like trying to thread a 
needle without an Aim or Accurate tag. 
 
Domain Tags (do not count towards the tag limit, you may only choose 1 per weapon) 
●​
Melee (brawl, adjacent): Basic melee weapon, like a blade or bludgeon. 
●​
Ranged (short, long): Basic ranged weapon, like a gun or thrown weapon. 
 
Damage Tags (does not count towards the tag limit, you may only choose 1 per weapon.) 
●​
Blunt: Hammers, shields, lead bullets, and striking staves. 
●​
Slash: Swords, knives, whips, and throwing blades. 
●​
Pierce: Rapiers, bullets, arrows, and stabbing knives. 
●​
-1 Explosive: Explosive rounds, grenades, missiles and explosive charges. 
●​
-2 Mystic: Choose one upon buying this tag:  
○​
Frost: raw cold, ice, water. 
○​
Flame: flames, light, raw heat. 
○​
Bolt: electricity, plasma, magnets. 
○​
Force: kinetic energy, wind, raw psychic force. 
○​
Rot: necrosis, acid, poison. 
 
+2 Tags (grants bonus points to spend on beneficial tags, can impose roll parameters.) 
1.​ Weak: Attacks with this weapon are always hard by default regardless of target. Exclusive with Strong. 
2.​ Fragile: Made of brittle or complex material that can shatter or stop working. Broken weapons are replaced during a rest. 
3.​ Set-Up: Requires active aid or set up by another player to properly use. 
4.​ Focus: Requires intense focus to use. Doing anything other than using this weapon grants the next attack with this weapon -1D. 
5.​ Cursed: When you roll no successes and at least one 1 on an attack with this weapon, take 1d3 stress. 
6.​ Sapping: 1/scene when you roll a 1 with this weapon, take 1d3 sin or lose a psyche burst. 
7.​ Dangerous: 1/scene when this weapon is used, the GM may tap this tag to reroll a risk die as long as it targets you, taking the second 
result as final. 
8.​ Heavy: Reduces your effective movement speed greatly while wielding this weapon. 
9.​ Scarce: Has finite uses before it is expended. Refreshed on a rest. Exclusive with Limited. 
 
+1 Tags (grants bonus points to spend on beneficial tags, can impose roll parameters.) 
1.​ 2H: Requires both hands to effectively wield, regardless of the wielder’s strength. 
2.​ Bulky: Is very large and cumbersome, and is hard to move fast or accurately. 
3.​ Limited: Has finite uses before it is expended, which can cause complications or require a turn to reload. Exclusive with Scarce. 
4.​ Messy: Deals heavy collateral damage and can cause unintended friendly fire or environmental complications. 
5.​ Clash: Can only be used in extremely close range, within personal space. Most often hand-to-hand combat. Exclusive with any tag that 
grants range. 
6.​ Aim: Can only be used at or beyond long range, making attacking a short range or closer target hard or impossible by default. Exclusive 
with any tag that grants melee. 
7.​ Conspicuous: Incredibly obvious while carried and used. Cannot be hidden, concealed, or stashed away. Draws attention easily. 
Exclusive with Pocket. 
8.​ Intense: Requires constant exertion and focus to wield effectively. Can throw its wielder around if not careful. 
9.​ Prep: Requires some sort of preparation to wield, and cannot be used to parry or respond to surprise attacks. 
 
-1 Tags 
1.​ Melee (short): Gains a chain or whip-like attachment that can be looped around things. 
2.​ Ranged (extreme): Gains a sniper-like range. Comes with a suppressor. 
3.​ Puncture: Specialized in puncturing through thick defenses and barriers. 
4.​ Slice: Specialized in cutting through physical materials much cleaner and easier than normal. 
5.​ Defensive: 1/scene this tag can reduce one instance of incoming stress by this weapon’s CAT. 
6.​ Batter: Specialized in rapid attacks and extremely quick wielding maneuvers. 
7.​ Proficient: 1/scene gain +1D on rolls with this weapon that utilize specific stances or techniques. 
8.​ Volatile: 1/scene this tag lets you flip a d2: on a success it deals 1 extra slashes, on a failure you take 1 stress. 
9.​ Silent: Specialized in quick takedowns, silent executions, and sneak attacks. 
 
-2 Tags 
1.​ Potent: Choose one blasphemy power, even one you do not have. 1/scene this tag lets you cast that blasphemy power as a weaker, 
diluted effect (-2 CAT) like a conduit. 
2.​ Pocket: Can be stored up a sleeve or in a pocket, regardless of size.  
3.​ Bloody: Rends armor and tears flesh far more violently than other weapons. Can cause grievous wounds. 
4.​ Cleaving: Deals much more damage against hordes of enemies. 
5.​ Punch: Carries significant stopping power and impact, and can easily throw even heavy things around. 
 


 
6.​ Oppressive: Can be used to disorient, grapple, pin down, and suppress movement. 
7.​ Accurate: Can be used to make trick shots, ricochets, and needle-threading. 
8.​ Parry: Built to disarm, deflect, or redirect attacks more easily, such as sword breakers and hooks. 
 
-X Tags 
1.​ Trick: Can swap between two sets of tags, each with a combined cost up to X following the max tag rules, 1/round before or after your 
action. Each set can have its own Domain and Damage tags. Only available to CAT3 and higher exo. 
 
 


 
Weapon Fuses (CAT3+): 
Weapon fuses follow the following rules: 
●​
Each weapon can only have one fuse, and each exorcist can only possess one fused weapon at a time.  
●​
Weapon fuses are only available to exorcists of CAT 3 or higher, cost 6 scrip, and increase the KP cost of a weapon by 1. 
●​
A weapon fuse can be taken off of any weapon and transferred to another between missions for free. You may swap the type of weapon 
fuse as well for 1 scrip. 
 
●​
Gachapon Tamagotchi 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. Weapon gains full sentience and can speak to you telepathically up to CAT distance, and out loud 
1/scene. Transferring this fuse does not cause the same personality to emerge. 1/rest you may gain +1D on one ridiculous or 
over-the-top shot or maneuver with it. 
●​
Friendship Bracelets 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. Can be called back to your hand by spending an action as long as there is a path for it to return, and 
cannot be moved by mundane means other than you (similar to Mjolnir). 
●​
Sealed Locket 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. The weapon itself completely phases through grace as if it didn’t exist, including blasphemies, traces 
and sins, and is immune to any sort of grace effect that would target it directly. 1/mission you may receive the same effect for one 
round, but you also lose the ability to see grace and graced creatures. 
●​
Bloody Bandage 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. This weapon can no longer harm creatures. The weapon becomes almost perfectly effective against 
physical materials. Blades can glide through steel like butter, bullets pass through concrete like jello, and hammers can flatten and 
punch through most materials. Any stress this weapon deals directly to beings is reduced to 0 as it phases through creatures 
harmlessly, which cannot be changed or modified by any effect in this book or another. 
●​
Family Photo 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. The weapon cannot be bent, broken, disabled, or in any way damaged by mundane means including 
impacts, weight, and explosives. If the weapon has the Fragile tag, it becomes incredibly brittle against supernatural effects. 
1/mission you may receive the same effect for one round, but you also lose the ability to move and think, essentially becoming an 
immovable frozen statue. You cannot end this effect early. 
●​
Memorabilia Charms 
○​
Fused to a ranged service weapon. You gain 3 special slugs you can fire that can easily blow through decently thick concrete and 
metal and deal +1 slashes against mundane humans. You may regain 1d3 slugs when you rest for 2KP. 
●​
Coffin Pendant 
○​
Fused to a non-consumable service weapon or kit. Pulling out this item costs ½ the amount of KP rounded down and can be 
hidden in otherwise conspicuous or impossible places. 
●​
Love Letter 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. 1/rest you may reduce your stress cap by 1d3 to deal equal slashes to an enemy talisman without 
rolling. This reduction goes away after a rest or you take an injury. 
●​
Novelty Keychain 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. 1/rest you may cause the weapon to grow to a frankly unreasonable size for a scene, increasing its 
CAT by 2 and giving it one additional +2 tag of your choice. 
●​
Emaciated Hand 
○​
Fused to a ranged weapon. You gain 3 special munitions that can curve around corners, ricochet, and home in on targets. These 
munitions always deal at least 1 slash and can hit at extreme distance. You can regain 1d3 munitions when you rest for 2KP. 
●​
Cool Paint 
○​
Fused to a service weapon or non-consumable kit item worth no more than 2 KP. The fused item can be instantly summoned to 
your hand from a sort of parallel space, but you cannot unsummon it unless you are touching it (no teleporting it back to your 
hand, essentially). If the weapon has charges or ammunition of some kind, it has enough for three individual uses. If summoning 
the item would cause it to intersect with something, it does, possibly embedding it in a surface or stabbing through enemies. 
●​
Fruit Stickers 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. Your weapon has three stickers on it. These stickers can be peeled off and placed on other surfaces, 
objects, or creatures. While attached, you gain the following effects: 
■​
If attached to a surface or object, you may attack “from” the sticker, acting as a new line of sight. If you cannot directly see 
where you are attacking, the roll is hard. 
■​
If attached to a creature or object, you may attack “at” the sticker, essentially instantly transmitting the attack up to a long 
distance to whatever the sticker is attached to. You still need to roll for the effects of the attack. 
The stickers do not regenerate until the end of the mission, and must be peeled back off and put on the weapon to reuse later. 
●​
Polished Dog Trinket 
○​
Fused to a service weapon. 1/rest your service weapon gains the ability to float and move within a short distance of you for a 
scene, moving with enough force to damage things. It will follow orders obediently but not intelligently, and grows slower and 
weaker the farther it is from you. It cannot deal damage to creatures while not wielded by you.  
 
 


 
ΤEMPERΔNCE 
The Lonely 
 
 
Temperance was discovered in the year ████ after their soul-domain intersected with the 
palace of exorcist E-2298. Following their discovery TEMERITY initiated astral projection 
contact to establish relationships and adopt them into the Virtue program. At first contact 
despite their supposed appearance as a ██ year old adult human ████, they spoke no 
language and instead communicated quite gratingly via direct emotion into the minds of 
people with the domain. 
 
An utter anomaly, Temperance was born with their body inside of their soul, rather than the 
other way around. Their lineage and place of birth is still unknown, as it is postulated their 
mother would never have carried them in pregnancy, instead being tethered by some astral 
umbilical cord to the infant. They do not seem to experience the phenomenon of aging due to 
time simply not existing in the Psychic Sea, nor do they have the need to sleep, or sustain 
themselves. CAIN regularly pays them in memories of fine cuisine, which Temperance 
accepts faster than any monetary donation.​
​
Temperance has formed a grace-woven pact, one that forces them to willingly allow CAIN 
use of their consistent and stable soul-domain as a sort of worldwide psychic hub for astral 
projection. Most CAIN admin have “permissions” at tiers that allow them to manipulate and 
arrange the malleable landscape of the domain. It is unknown if Temperance could revoke 
these permissions without the consent of Admin. Temperance is simply glad to have 
company. 
 
Likes: 
●​
Weird exorcists 
●​
ADMIN Budgeted Solitary Time 
●​
The memory of a fine meal 
Dislikes: 
●​
CAIN ADMIN 
●​
Prudence’s presence 
●​
Others tampering with their landscaping 
Favourite Food: 
●​
The memory of Salisbury Steak 
 
Drive: 
Uniquely, Cathedral does not require a direct bond with Temperance, only instruction from them and the development of one’s own soul. Exorcists 
gain a new unique agenda item when creating their character, called their Drive. This does not count towards their maximum agenda items. This is 
a simple, short one-sentence goal or ideal the exorcist strives towards. To make discerning it easier, answer the following questions: 
●​
What does your character think they want? 
●​
What does your character really want, deep down? 
●​
What does your character think they need? 
●​
What does your character really need, deep down? 
Whenever an exorcist makes notable or sizable progress towards achieving this goal, at the end of the hunt they may increase their Cathedral 
bond by one. If they make important or critical choices antithetical to their goal, they gain 1 xp at the end of the mission and do not increase their 
bond level. 
 
In agreement with your GM, design a stricture for your character that challenges them, often related to their Drive. This character cannot attempt 
an action against said restriction and/or rolls 0d on checks relating to it. This should be a challenge, and not something that flows easily with the 
character’s personality. If you want, you may simply replicate the stricture of another Virtue. 
 
Stricture: 
Ignore for one roll by taking 1D3 nonlethal stress.​
You can never enter into a private area owned by an individual unless invited, and must obey when ordered to leave from private places owned by 
an individual. Ownership by larger entities like companies and associations is exempt from this limitation. 
 
Bond: 
●​
Level 0: When you are invited beyond a threshold by its owner, you gain an immediate knowledge of the layout and map of the area in an 
up to CAT distance around you. 
●​
Level 1: You gain the ability to see an abstract haze of mundane humans’ inner worlds in a CAT area around you. You can glean 
emotional state, health, and abstract prominent thoughts, such as longing for someone, confusion about an event, or anger at work, but 
cannot glimpse any fine details beyond a few syllables. Large crowds make this ability hazy and hard to use. 
●​
Level 2: Gain the ability to use the Cathedral High Blasphemy, and an activating gesture and/or phrase. 
●​
Level 3: You gain the ability to empower your Cathedral. You may spend an advancement to increase the size of the Cathedral by one 
CAT or increase the talisman by 1 (up to 2 times each), and you may spend two advancements to add one free feature to your Cathedral 
(up to 2 times) or swap one feature for another one (any number of times). 
 
If relevant, you can with the GM’s permission gain a level in the bond mid-session rather than during downtime or after hunts, which allows for 
dramatic “Cathedral Awakening” moments. 
 
 
 


 
Tormented Inner World: Cathedral 
When you take this High Blasphemy, you must choose to replace one of the Blasphemies you already know.  
Choose one of the following Forms: 
1.​ Dungeon. No being can willingly enter or exit the Cathedral. All damage done to the environment stays within the manifested 
environment, and vanishes when dismissed or shattered. Can be more alien and unnatural in layout and nature compared to reality. +1 to 
Cathedral Talisman. You may have the edge of the space act as a solid barrier or a spatial loop. 
2.​ Stage. All beings can willingly enter or exit the Cathedral by taking 1d3 nonlethal stress or slashes on their talisman, or without cost if you 
choose. The manifested environment overlaps with the real world and in general is more bound by the laws of reality, merely adding 
effects overtop of it. +1 free Feature choice. Shattering a Stage Cathedral deals half the stress and slashes, but still deals 1d6 sin to you. 
Choose one of the following Features for free, and one as a dark power: 
1.​ Ward: Name a specific feature of the environment. 1/round one exorcist or ally may take advantage of this feature to fully ignore a relevant 
complication. 
2.​ Edge: Name a specific feature of the environment. 1/round one exorcist or ally may take advantage of this feature to give themselves +1D 
on a roll. 
3.​ Buffer: Name a specific feature of the environment. 1/scene one exorcist or ally may take advantage of this feature to subtract ½ 
(rounded up) from any stress they take, minimum of 1. This effect refreshes whenever an exorcist takes an injury or enters brink of death. 
4.​ Totem: Name a specific feature of the environment. 1/scene you may invoke that feature to reroll any risk dice, choosing the higher result. 
5.​ Trick: Describe the spatial nature of the environment. 1/round one exorcist or ally may increase or decrease their relative distance to 
another being, down to adjacent and up to long nearly instantly after any reaction or action, even if it is not their turn. 
6.​ Spiral: Describe the spatial nature of the environment. No being within the Cathedral takes damage from falling, and all exorcists and 
allies have greatly increased mobility (running along walls like floors, jumping higher and/or between pillars, gliding short distances, etc). 
7.​ Chaos: Describe the energy of the environment. It can now possess hazardous or difficult terrain which affects yourself, allies, and 
enemies equally. 1/scene one exorcist or ally may take advantage of this effect to increase their slashes dealt by 1, which refreshes 
whenever an exorcist or ally rolls two or more sixes in one roll.   
8.​ Font: Describe the energy of the environment. You gain special psyche charges equal to the number of psyche bursts spent on the 
Cathedral. Once per round, one exorcist other than yourself may expend a charge to use a Blasphemy power at CAT+2 without spending 
a psyche burst of their own.  
When summoning your Cathedral you may activate all free Features for no cost other than expending psyche bursts, but you may also choose to 
activate the dark power by taking 1d6 sin. The name, shape, layout, terrain, Form, and free Features of the Cathedral must be chosen when first 
taking this power, and cannot be changed unless you are affected by the ten-day or greater VEIL side effect. You may change the dark power 
feature any time you activate it. 
 
This power can very easily affect the parameters of rolls while in the area. Additional effects are up to the GM and can be improvised depending 
on the fiction; these can be both positive and detrimental effects based on the nature of the Cathedral. 
 
CATHEDRAL 
Scene, Curse, Self, CAT range 
Prerequisites: CAT II Exorcist 
As an action, spend all remaining Psyche Bursts and target a number of beings with a combined CAT equal to yours (A willing target counts as 
CAT0). You must include yourself. Your Grace manifests into an extruded cyst-dimension within your Soul, with a talisman equal to your PSYCHE, 
and an effective internal distance of PSYCHE+1. All targeted beings are sucked into the cyst-dimension instantaneously. As part of this action, you 
must perform either a triggering phrase (like a spell or the name of the Cathedral) or a triggering gesture (like hand signs or a stance) or both. 
●​
Any graced enemy may make an attack against the Cathedral as a reaction, which can be intercepted by a willing exorcist who is able to, 
taking the full stress as normal in its place. If no exorcist intercepts or is unable to, the enemy deals 1 slash to the Cathedral’s talisman.  
●​
If the Cathedral's talisman fills, it shatters violently and all occupants are ejected crudely into realspace, taking 1d6 stress or slashes on 
their talisman. You take an additional 1d6 sin as the cyst in your soul ruptures, and you lose the ability to summon the Cathedral until the 
end of the next rest. 
●​
If you dismiss the Cathedral willingly, it instantly demanifests and all occupants are harmlessly transported back to the real world to the 
closest available spot. 
If you meet your sin cap or overflow while within the Cathedral, the overflow is postponed and SIN KEEPS ACCRUING UNTIL YOU LEAVE THE 
CATHEDRAL. This can cause you to resolve several overflows at once at the end of the scene. If you would Give Up or Die while within the 
Cathedral, you do not turn into an Imago or die and instead irreparably tear spacetime in a 1/2 CAT area around yourself, instantly killing all 
mundane humans in the area and causing massive damage. 
Only one Cathedral may be active in the same given space. Whenever a Cathedral would be summoned within an existing one, or 
two would overlap, the owners of both must either decide which to keep or engage in Psyche Clash. The loser suffers 1d3 sin and 
their Cathedral is overwritten.  
 
 


 
 
D1LIGENCE-02 
The Smith 
 
Diligence was apprehended and brought into CAIN in the year ████ after extensive reports of 
animate and irregular objects beyond the normal scope of a Sin’s influence were reported in 
███████, Michigan. After their apprehension, to which they objected violently, they had 
resisted all known forms of conditioning or reeducation. Repeated Veil usage was denied due 
to how quickly it burns through Hopes.  
 
Diligence’s high blasphemy, Auteur, allowed them to tear away scraps of a target’s soul and 
imbue it into objects with some degree of control over its resulting effects. This included their 
own soul which they had (until incident T-0001) showed no sign of tampering with. 
 
Despite their quite vocal deriding of CAIN, and CASTLE in particular, they had shown to be 
much more cooperative when given “creative liberties” as far as they were aware. Sadly such 
creative liberties led to incident T-0001, in which Diligence themselves was presumed ████ 
and in their place was born Diligence-02, a standard plated armor set of polished black metal 
that is now ██████████ with the entirety of Diligence’s soul. They refer to themselves 
separately from Diligence-01 despite being objectively the same continuous consciousness, 
and displays an irregularly scattered form of amnesia that seems to have discarded core 
memories at random. 
 
Diligence-02 is much more passive and controllable in their current state, and has shown to 
be accepting of reeducation and conditioning. They now toil away in their forge and workshop 
with their former (now solid stone) body held aloft on a pedestal in the centre of the 
warehouse. They are often observed on camera touching the hand of the statue as one would 
a mirror, but denies it confusedly upon interrogation.

●​
Classical film directors 
●​
The smell of leather 
●​
Hope’s voice, for some reason 
Dislikes: 
●​
Interruptions to their work 
●​
Fortitude breaking their things 
●​
Having to mass produce items 
Favourite Food: 
●​
Formerly, apple crisp 
 
Stricture: 
Ignore for one roll by taking 1D3 nonlethal stress.​
You can never do anything less than perfect. You always roll 0d when taking the easiest or laziest option, rather than doing a task thoroughly. 
 
Bond: 
●​
Level 0: 1/mission you may touch an object and take up to 6 stress to learn an equal amount of years of its life story, possibly including 
where, when and by who it was made, who has owned or used it over the years, and dominant emotional events surrounding it. 
●​
Level 1: Gain the ability to learn the Auteur high blasphemy. 
●​
Level 2: Gain +1D when working with finely crafted items, such as intricate tomes, engraved metalwork, sculpture, complicated 
locksmithing, etc. 
●​
Level 3: Your usage of the Auteur high blasphemy no longer lowers your sin cap, and you may roll PSYCHE to tear a scrap from another 
being’s soul instead, only spending psyche bursts on at least one success and dealing slashes to their talisman equal to the number of 
successes. 
 
 


 
Magnum Opus: Auteur 
The Auteur high blasphemy works differently compared to most other High Blasphemies. It is a drastically more freeform and narrative ability, and 
should only be given out to players who you trust will abide by the limits of the fiction they are in. Retcons of Mementos are discouraged, but 
rebalances or reworks of Mementos after-the-fact should be expected if needed. 
 
At its base Auteur allows players to effectively enchant or alter objects in a way that makes them into irregularities. An exorcist can only create 
one Memento per downtime between hunts, and its specifications must follow the following rules: 
●​
The Memento’s three specifications must have a combined CAT equal to (your CAT × 2) + 1, with any one specification being no higher 
than your CAT+1. You can count your CAT as lower for the purposes of this effect, down to CAT1. The Memento becomes a unique 
Graced Object with a CAT equal to the one you counted as while making it. 
●​
The Memento must have an effect loosely associated with the perception of that object in the cultural subconscious; such as a stapler 
pinning people to a surface, a pot being unable to rise in temperature while you are looking at it, a refrigerator perfectly preserving 
whatever is put inside of it, or a hammer that flattens objects regardless of how malleable they are. 
●​
The Memento cannot cause direct slashes or stress to objects or creatures until CAT4+ in potency, and even then is still considered a 
“mundane” effect and attacking sins with it is hard. 
●​
The Memento is made with a piece of your soul (until Bond Stage 3) and as such only really listens to you. Anyone you do not grant 
permission to cannot benefit from it. At Bond Stage 3, permission automatically extends to the source of the soul-scrap too, if they are 
alive. 
●​
The Memento can easily affect the parameters of a roll when appropriate. Additional effects are up to the GM and can be improvised 
depending on the fiction. It is highly recommended you work with the Admin to make a tailored “special ability” appropriate for the item, 
to make it unique.

AUTEUR 
Instant, Transmute, Touch 
 
1/mission spend all remaining psyche bursts. You tear away a scrap of your soul, lowering your sin cap by half until the end of the next rest and 
generating a Memento from an object you can touch according to the Memento guidelines detailed above. This transmutation is permanent, and 
you lose a single important memory of your choice as collateral for stuffing a piece of your soul into an object. ​
 
You may bring up to 1 Memento you have previously created along with you on a hunt by expending KP equal to 1/2 its CAT, as long as the 
Memento is able to be carried in two hands. 
 
Whenever you or an ally attempts to make use of the Memento, roll PSYCHE: 
●​
You gain +1D if it is an object you handcrafted. On at least one success, you gain 2 “bonus dice” and 1 additional one for each success. 
You can spend a bonus die to add +1D6 to any roll (yours or an ally’s) before or after the roll happens. Unused bonus dice vanish at the 
start of the next rest. The Memento cannot be reactivated until the end of the next rest, and unless you spend at least all psyche bursts 
again to refuel it, at which point you may roll PSYCHE again. 
●​
If no successes are rolled, the Memento throws a tantrum. You do not gain the advantage dice unless you immediately take 1d3 stress 
trying to wrangle the Memento into behaving. 1/mission the Admin can choose to inflict the effect of the upset Memento on a target of 
their choice within its AOE, which can possibly cause harm to innocents. 
●​
If the Memento is a weapon, you can still use it as a simple mundane weapon of CAT power. 
​
You cannot create a Memento of the same object twice. Once you make a scissors memento, that is the only scissors memento you will ever be 
able to make, etc.
 
 


 
PROV!DENCE 
The Inheritor 
 
Providence is CAIN’s most recent upstart to join the Virtue Programme, infused with a deluded 
sense of invulnerability and importance. It is unknown where they were originally born or came 
from, but they look vaguely ███ ███████. They are an extremely difficult entity to track but 
through a series of one-sided deals and paranaturally enforced oaths Providence is entirely 
unable to leave the supervision of at least 1 AUTHORITY Admin at all times unless given a 
specific time window of freedom.  
 
Providence’s high blasphemy Stairwell is unique in the sense that it is not intended to be a 
catastrophic power, but simply results in obliterating large amounts of land in the process of its 
completion. The current Providence is the only known exorcist to have taken the Fifth Step 
and survived, immunizing them against direct exposure to the psychic sea. As such they are 
practically immune to sin events and can utilize the “Spiral Stairway” as they describe it to 
traverse abstract distances. Their power seems to aggravate the Spindle, but has so far not 
enticed any punishment. 
 
Providence is very vain, and very slippery. They believe nearly everything they see is theirs, in 
an odd or convoluted way. They do not desire things for their material value as much as their 
intrinsic properties, for possessing things humanity cannot. Envying diamonds for their lustre 
and resilience, the tree for its age and self-sufficiency, a bird for its freedom and flight. It is 
confirmed that Providence possesses a Full Occlusion Threshold in the psychic sea which 
contains the entirety of their personal hoard, but AUTHORITY has so far been unable to 
convince or deceive them into opening the threshold, or locate it without their knowledge.​
​

●​
Shiny things, particularly metals. Does not need to be valuable. 
●​
Direct open-air sunlight. 
Dislikes: 
●​
Sitting still. 
●​
Faith. 
●​
Having to work to earn things. 
Favourite Food: 
●​
Lemon-lime biscuits. 
 
Stricture: 
Ignore for one roll by taking 1D3 nonlethal stress.​
You are impossibly vain. Roll 0d on any roll to empathize with, display kindness to, aid, or protect mundane humans. 
 
Bond: 
●​
Level 0: 1/scene your movement cannot be stopped by any mundane means, including mundane creatures, vehicles, doors, and walls up 
to a half a metre thick. 
●​
Level 1: Gain the ability to learn the Stairwell high blasphemy up to the fourth step. 
●​
Level 2: You can warp yourself up to a half a metre at will in any direction as an action, as long as you can see the destination. 
●​
Level 3. You may take the fifth step. 
 
 


 
Winding Ascendant Path: Stairwell 
Stairwell initiates the users on their path towards utter divinity, essentially allowing them to approach a form that could feasibly survive within the 
depths of the Psychic Sea without succumbing to the emotional lethality. The pure force exuded by the transmutation is enough to crack reality 
itself in a wide berth around the user the further along they get into their travel. 
 
 
STAIRWELL 
Instant, Self 
 
Initiate your divine inheritance. You may descend one step per use. If used again during the same mission, you return to the last step you took and 
you may not take lower steps again once they have been taken. Spend a Psyche Burst for each step you take, and you may roll for 1d6 sin to take 
a step instead one time. Stress dealt applies to allies as well, though they take 1 less. You and any allies killed by this ability to not get the 
on-death Departed or Deserter agenda. 
 
●​
Egy, The First Step: Deal 1 slash to all creatures within adjacent distance. Take 1 stress.  
○​
Instantly kills mundane beings.  
○​
The ground cracks under your feet, and the air hums. 
●​
Kettő, The Second Step: Deal 2 slashes to all creatures within a short distance. Take 1d3 stress. 
○​
Objects and creatures CAT2 size or smaller within a short distance of you are flung away rapidly.  
○​
Gravity seems to increase.  
●​
Három, The Third Step: Deal 3 slashes to all creatures within a long distance. Take 2d3 stress.  
○​
Instantly kills traces and drifters, regardless of their talisman.  
○​
Anything not sturdily built within a long distance of you crumbles immediately, and even heavy objects are flung away from you 
like paper.  
●​
Négy, The Fourth Step: Deal 4 slashes to all creatures within an extreme distance. Take 2d6 stress. 
○​
If this would leave exactly 1 slash on an enemy’s talisman, it instantly kills them. 
○​
Buildings, landscapes, and even the winds are crushed, cracked, and torn asunder in a long distance around you. You are docked 
any pay as compensation for damage control. 
○​
Any creature not blown away finds gravity too strong to stand up in unless blessed with extreme strength. 
●​
Őt, The Fifth Step: You reach the divine. You instantly die and vaporize, and cannot Defy Fate or be revived. Your character is lost 
forever, which cannot be changed by any rule in this book or another. 
○​
Instantly kill one enemy of your choice, permanently, as far as the horizon, so long as you have directly encountered them in battle 
at least one time this hunt. This cannot be avoided or undone by any rule in this book or another. 
○​
The area in a short distance around your death is obliterated, flattened, and compacted to a perfect polished mirror-like level, long 
distance is heavily damaged, and extreme distance looks as if hit by a minor earthquake. 
○​
All exorcists involved are docked pay for damage control. 
 
 
 


 
JOY(?) 
The Lover 
 
Joy is a unique figure. None of their biographical records are considered relevant anymore, 
and have been marked as effectively obsolete. Every time they use their high blasphemy 
Adoration, their memories and personality change to accommodate it until their trigger word is 
enacted. They do not consider their mind or self as a “part of them”, and see their 
consciousness as a facet to some form of higher definition of existence. It is theorized that 
they quite simply do not have a “soul” in the psychic sea as most beings do, and instead are 
able to draw in the souls of others to serve as their own for a time. It is unknown if they retain 
the ability to assume previously adopted identities, and they refuse to experiment on the issue. 
 
Due to the deep and unwieldy nature of their power, they cannot be considered a single 
individual and if they are compelled to escape, one would find them very hard to pin down. Joy 
has been found missing over 72 times while retreating to deep cover, each requiring a mass 
subliminal message front to reveal and track them down. Despite their reluctance to 
cooperate, their skills at imitation and compulsory subjugation are an indisputably vital asset 
when working with high attention targets. 
 
It is impossible to tell whether Joy has assumed a new form, and a default of 1 mandated 
trigger exposure per 7 days is mandated to reveal and ensure that they are where they say 
they are. While Joy themselves has been conditioned into obedience, any non-CAIN 
personnel identity they wear has not. The longer they remain in deep cover, the harder and 
harder it gets to pull them back.​
​

●​
Extremely detailed cosplay. 
●​
Operatic Theatre. 
●​
Spy thrillers. 
Dislikes: 
●​
Quiet spaces. 
●​
Their own blood. 
●​
Thinking for too long. 
Favourite Food: 
●​
Variable. 
 
Stricture: 
Ignore for one roll by taking 1D3 nonlethal stress.​
Your heart bleeds for others. You roll 0d on actions made to abandon or leave behind those in great need, and are haunted by those you have 
failed. 
 
Bond: 
●​
Level 0: Your face becomes incredibly nondescript; both allies and others alike have trouble recalling anything notable about you. 
●​
Level 1: You gain access to the Infatuation high blasphemy. 
●​
Level 2: If you use Infatuation on an allied exorcist, you suffer no ill effects upon returning, and gain their blasphemy and agenda abilities 
as well. 
●​
Level 3: You gain access to the Adoration effect. 
 
 


 
Ego Death: Infatuation 
Infatuation brings the wielder’s desire into reality, transforming them into the very embodiment of envy. This is not a simple imitation, but a perfect 
one, the abandoning of oneself to become another entirely. Walk a mile in another’s shoes, but best remember to never forget your own. 
 
INFATUATION 
Instant, Short 
 
Fall deep into another. As part of this action, target a living human or exorcist within range: 
●​
If the target is unwilling, you must roll PSYCHE, only spending all your psyche bursts on at least one success. On a failure, you slip into a 
coma until the start of the next scene. 
●​
If the target is a mundane human, you may choose to either kill them instantly and erase them from reality, or spare them and slip them 
into a coma. If the target is not a mundane human they fall into a coma regardless and no harm can come to them in this state. 
●​
Once a target is chosen, choose a “trigger”. This can be something like an image, phrase, or sound, but has to be recognizable as a 
distinct trigger, and not just garbled pixels or noise. 
 
For the duration, your self and body are effectively perfectly replaced with your target’s, essentially becoming a perfect persona of them. You are 
now, for all intents and purposes, your target. This includes memories, habits, personality traits, and ways of thought. You lose all memories, 
hooks, and afflictions from your previous self, minus the “trigger” implanted deep within your consciousness. The GM can guide you on what your 
target would do occasionally if needed, and supply info the target would know when needed. You do not inherently trust allied exorcists if you have 
them unless your target already did. 
 
If your target has an execution talisman, you gain their talisman as your own and inherit any damage they’ve taken. This effect lasts until the 
“trigger” is shown or spoken to you or you “die”, causing you to revert back to your normal state. If you died or chose to spare the target, you take 
an injury upon returning. If your target was in a coma due to this ability, they wake up once you return to normal. 
ADORATION 
Cling tighter to the mask. You may choose to either accept or deny this power once you reach bond level 3 with Joy. If accepted, you cannot be rid 
of this power. 
 
As part of the Infatuation high blasphemy, you may now target any dead human or exorcist whose body is at least 50% intact, or any previous 
identity you have consumed. When replicating a deceased target, you lose any memories within 12 hours of your time of death, and wake up with 
extreme fatigue and nausea until the start of the next scene. There is no time limit on the age of the corpse. 
 
However: All uses of Infatuation become semi-permanent, requiring not just a trigger but a deep and genuine connection to something to pull you 
out of the persona. Even if you return, you return fragmented and mixed with whatever memories the persona had; different memories and 
personality traits will resurface each time. At the end of the mission, you may make up to CAT “new characters” to represent these different 
personas, even if the target was not a graced individual. Their CAT is equal to yours, and they have the same amount of blasphemy powers and 
agenda abilities that you do. You may choose to begin a mission as any of these characters.  
 
 


 
KINDNΞSS 
The Weary 
 
Kindness is the singular most restricted individual within the ranks of CAIN. Their power has 
no direct use in deployment, and as such they are kept almost entirely in cryostasis unless a 
situation permits their summoning. They have not been active for more than 5 hours in a row 
in nearly 118 years, and have never seen anything of the outside world during any of those 5 
hours aside from the same interior facility hallways. They have shown no attempt to escape or 
resist treatment and as such are permitted to walk without restraints or armed personnel 
escorts. 
 
The existence of Kindness and their power is reserved solely for potential candidates, 
including utilization of Hope’s Blood for thorough memetic containment. Their power is much 
more philosophically terrifying than most Virtues’, as unlike the more present and powerful 
effects of other High Blasphemies, Respite has much more ontologically insidious implications 
as to the nature of sentient existence. TEMERITY is unsure whether the realm attributed to 
Kindness is an afterlife or merely a very competent mirror, and until such theories of an 
afterlife are disproven TEMERITY has speculated the possible reduction in exorcist population 
is worth the containment procedures they have enacted.  
 
Kindness does not like most current standing members of CAIN, and treats their designation 
with particular venom. They are an exceedingly nihilistic individual who fully believes that CAIN 
as an organization is irreparably doomed to fail within the coming years, though they cannot 
have possibly been involved in any such foretelling. Their long cryostasis cycles have 
prevented them from forming any meaningful bonds with anyone outside of their realm, and 
they no longer take much interest in the living. ​
​

N/A: Incapable of consumption 
 
Stricture: 
Ignore for one roll by taking 1D3 nonlethal stress.​
You are no longer your own. You roll 0D on actions that purposefully prioritize yourself over others. 
 
Bond: 
●​
Level 0: Your threshold for instant death increases to 9 stress. 
●​
Level 1: You may automatically infer the cause of death of any corpse you can touch, including whether or not the death was supernatural 
in origin and the time of death. 
●​
Level 2: You gain access to the Respite High Blasphemy. 
●​
Level 3: You gain access to Respite’s evolution, Deliverance.  
 
 


 
Realm of the Immortal Soul: Respite 
Respite allows its user to enter the “afterlives” (heavy quotes) of a corpse, essentially calling upon their dormant soul. Whether this realm truly 
exists outside of this High Blasphemy, and whether the soul truly is back from the dead or merely a reflection, is unclear. 
 
RESPITE 
Instant, Curse, Self 
 
You immediately and voluntarily suffer instant death, which cannot be avoided and cannot trigger sin overflow. You do not trigger any abilities that 
rely on exorcist deaths, such as the Guardian agenda. There is no time limit or maximum duration of this ability, and while you are dead it is 
impossible to contact you or bring you out of the effect unless by Black Blood medicine, the Desecrate power from Jaunt, or other revival effects. 
Otherwise, you are completely cut off from the outside world. At any time while this power is active, you may choose to resuscitate yourself, 
reentering your corpse and coming back to life. If your body sustained severe damage while you were gone, you must immediately enter sin 
overflow. Otherwise, you return to life with a vague feeling of numbness and intense shivers. 
 
As part of this power, you may target a corpse you can touch. If you do, when you are dead you may enter a reflection of the corpse’s 
consciousness in a psychic realm, essentially summoning their soul back from the dead. While within this psychic realm, you may speak to the 
consciousness. They are exactly as they were in life, at the moment of their death. They don’t have a perfect memory, and only remember what 
they could have reasonably remembered at their time of death, but can be coaxed into remembering by manipulating the realm and rolling 
PSYCHE. They are vaguely aware of the time that has passed and the fact that they are dead, but aren’t sure if they’ve spent it in this realm. 
Reviving yourself causes you to eject from this realm, forcing you to take 1d3 sin upon awakening. 
 
DELIVERANCE 
You may now willingly enter the realm of your own soul during death, and may invite any soul you converse with while dead to join you. If they do, 
they reside in your personal “afterlife” forever, rather than their own. You may visit and talk to any soul you have Delivered unto your realm when 
you suffer instant death or when you use Respite, and may take one soul from your realm into the one of a corpse you target as a guest. 
 
Black Blood Medicine no longer revives you, but returning to your body no longer triggers sin overflow unless 50% or more of it is obliterated.  
 
It is unclear what the fate of your soul, your realm, or any of the souls within it is if you ever permanently die, as Respite users cannot be targeted 
by other users of Respite. Best not to find out.
 
 


 
WISD()M 
The Silent 
 
Wisdom can hardly be considered a thinking being at their advanced stage. Due to the nature 
of their power, and the extreme lengths to which CAIN utilizes it, they are rendered comatose 
and insensate within the bowels of THRONE-1A. Cryogenic preservation has been enacted to 
prevent deterioration of their body while their mind races and overflows with information. 
Future Wisdom bond applicants must show prowess with Ping in order to succeed the current 
Wisdom when the previous inevitably degrades. 
 
Wisdom’s power is the Ping, the embodiment of the anxious and innate desire to know if 
others truly think of you. Anyone that knows their name or true face is immediately registered 
in their mind as a visual and informational display of who and where they are. As their power 
expanded and progressed, they slowly learned to apply this hyper-awareness to things beyond 
themselves, providing CAIN with the ability to surpass the Heisenberg Uncertainty Principle 
and rocket technology far beyond what the wider world currently employs. However, a fatal 
misapplication of their power overloaded their brain, rendering them inert.  
 
However, their power has thankfully not deactivated alongside their thoughts. CAIN has seen 
fit to use their still-notified mind by inserting their name as an obfuscated keyword in all major 
CAIN documents and media, extracting information from their brain into a globe to monitor the 
position of anyone with knowledge of CAIN documents across the world. A nearly 
unnoticeable memetic tracer, and a perfect one at that. If unsanctioned dots appear, a 
surveillance team is immediately summoned. If they find any local evidence of awareness of 
CAIN, a strike team is immediately called in to eliminate and Hope is immediately requested 
for a local wipe. Wisdom’s former teammates have been wiped by Hope. None remember their 
name. ​
​
Likes: 
●​
Irrelevant 
Dislikes: 
●​
Irrelevant 
Favourite Food: 
●​
Irrelevant 
 
Stricture: 
None may ever know you.  
Every time a new being sees your face or learns your real name, you immediately take 1d3 stress and roll 1 less rest dice during the next rest as 
you struggle to tune out the new information. If a large group of beings learns all at once, you take 2d3 stress instead and must spend the next rest 
with an agonizing migraine, rolling a single 1d2 instead.  
 
Bond: 
●​
Level 0: You may become instantly aware of the vague (long distance radius circle) location of any being that knows your name or has 
seen your face as long as they are thinking about you. There is no range limit on where this notification comes from, only a limit on its 
accuracy. 
●​
Level 1: You gain constant Awareness of the number and direction of every graced mortal within a long distance. 
●​
Level 2: You gain access to the Ping High Blasphemy, which you can use once per mission safely. 
●​
Level 3: Your Awareness now extends to mundane humans and weaker supernatural creatures such as traces and small drifters. Each 
count and distance is grouped separately. 
 
 


 
Ever-Staring Eyes: Ping 
Ping allows its user to become perfectly aware of their surroundings to an absurd degree. In order to prevent losing their mind and going 
comatose from the raw information, they must filter out unknowable amounts of information in an attempt to see anything at all. 
 
PING 
Scene, Charm, Self 
 
Spend all remaining psyche bursts. For the duration, you become instantly aware of the entirety of your surroundings. This grants the following 
effects: 
-​
You gain perfect knowledge of the shape of every surface in a ½ CAT distance area, including things inside of walls, underground, and 
inside of objects. You also learn the position of any moving or living thing in the instant this snapshot is taken. 
-​
You gain a rough understanding of every surface within a CAT distance area, learning the vague exposed topology as blurry silhouettes. 
-​
You no longer need line of sight for any action as long as unmoving inanimate objects are between you and your target, gaining the ability 
to arc and bounce attacks and focus off of surfaces with ease. After a round has passed, this fades unless you can reasonably infer the 
position of a moving target with the unmoving surfaces, making the roll hard. After three rounds have passed, this fades completely. 
 
You may refresh this snapshot at any moment to re-learn the position of moving and living things by taking 1d3 stress as an action within the same 
scene. You have a constant +1D to any actions that take advantage of the topology of unmoving terrain and objects you know of for as long as this 
power is active. 
 
Each time you use this power during a mission beyond the first, you immediately take an injury as the mental blockade in your brain begins to 
buckle against the pressure of pure information.

16.​Eel 
17.​Retainer 
18.​Layabout 
19.​Airhead 
20.​Archivist 
21.​Tower 
22.​Wizard 
23.​Tourist 
24.​Cuckoo 
25.​Wheel 
26.​Dragon 
27.​Deserter
