Do note that this is simply a measure of the presence and type of grace in an area, not its direct effects. This is due to not only emotion but also 
thoughts, memories, and intent determining the effect that high pressure fields of grace exert on their surroundings, making studying the effects 
much more of an anthropological and psychological task than a purely physical one. 
 
The Question of Instrumentality arises from the fact that measurements of grace fields outside the scope of normal human influence seem to 
portray a monumental drop in background grace pressure, suggesting that either the psychic sea only exists within the sphere of human influence 
or that the human mind possess some unique property that “thins” the barrier of reality during collision with the psychic sea. The density of human 
settlement on earth and its orbit is too dense to properly formulate a non-polluted experiment, and as such this question has gone long 
unanswered.  
 
Note: TEMERITY has recently had their application to influence the Second Space Race approved. Many TEMERITY occultist are joyous at the 
prospect of a potential future abdelometer and pathometer setup being sent with the next Mars Rover to confirm or deny this long debate. 
On the Nature of the Psychic Sea: 
 
The Psychic Sea is an imaginary other-realm that lacks the classical definitions of time and space that our world adheres to. Nonetheless, it is 
verifiably its own plane with its own laws and life and nature, but is simply so alien to us that mere contact with the raw void of the Psychic Sea is 
instantly fatal to mundane humans, and harmful to exorcists and grace users.  
 
A Sin Event is the best example of this phenomenon: the micro-instant of a burst of raw Psychic Sea that floods in due to the puncture wound of a 
sin event immediately causes all forms of organic life to die; plants, animals, fungi, bacterium, and humans all seem equally affected by this death. 
This death is seemingly caused by a simple de-animation at a cellular level, not mutating or fraying but simply stopping the chemical processes by 
which cells live. 
 
The Psychic Sea can never be entered directly, due to both its lethal nature and the cloth that is existence barring our exit, but it can be intruded 
upon by extruding extremely thin Thresholds such as Temperance’s Domain to the point where its laws begin to enact themselves on mundane 
reality without the lethality of direct contact. It should be noted that this effect does not affect atoms so much as raw reality itself; Thresholds are 
repeatedly and ostensibly known for violating the currently accepted laws of thermodynamics by creating and destroying matter and energy to suit 
the Threshold’s ambient effect. Such Thresholds are even more prone to obeying the imagination and cultural whims of humanity, and naturally 
occurring thresholds due to the irregularity in reality’s cloth are the most commonly accepted “haunted” or nonsensical places.  
On Thresholds and Plasticity: 
 
Much like how positive pressure increases the push of the Psychic Sea into reality and forms grace, negative pressure draws reality into the 
Psychic Sea. This inverse “suction” increases the “plasticity” of reality in its vicinity, allowing psychoemotional manipulation of the environment on 
a macroscopic level. The most common expressions of Thresholds are area-of-effect Sin domains, such as the virulent calcifying fog of the Ogre, 
or the reality-warping of the Lord. Some sins have higher capabilities for Threshold generation based on AMBER signature.  
 
Note: Higher spikes in frequencies between the Ennui and Repulsion poles are almost directly correlated with the potential for threshold 
generation, hereby designated as “Desire Potency”. Lords possess the highest Desire Potency of all sins. 
 


 
 
Plasticity is best described as the “distance” in which an area is “pushed” into the psychic sea by grace expenditure. The more intense the grace 
expenditure in abdels, the further the pocket balloons into the unreality. Most notably, blasphemies operate by an exorcist’s sinseed extruding its 
own temporary threshold strong enough to warp reality to their desired effect. Such thresholds cause significant strain on the exorcist’s soul, and 
prolonged usage is not recommended. 
 
Full Occlusion Thresholds occur when the warping seals an entire pocket of space off from reality, essentially creating an isolated bubble of 
temperate reality inside the otherwise lethal psychic sea. Such palaces are incredibly hard to create and maintain safely but if sustained allow 
manipulation and modification of reality on an unprecedented level. 
 
Note: TEMERITY has spent nearly 329 assets attempting to recreate the effect of a Sin’s Palace, the foremost example and only available 
definition of Full Occlusion Thresholds. Given the training time of assets needed to approach the skill needed to summon one, expending any 
more has been deemed unwise. 
 
Addendum: The recent discovery of Temperance has sparked newly enlightened intrigue in the halls of the Covens. AUTHORITY approval 
regarding further asset expenditure pending. 
 
It is most important to note that this branch of paraphysics serves the purpose of determining the causes of supernatural events, not their effects. 
Due to the close ties the supernatural has with human cognition and emotion, measuring effects by any categorical means is utterly pointless 
given they are as varied as the human subjective experience. It is vaguely possible to identify common signatures via their resultant effect, but 
determining the actual mechanics by which grace operates in real time is futile. 
SEER Irregularity Potency Classification: 
 
Every irregularity is assigned a Potency Category based on a formula computing its EAGP over its falloff radius. 
 
CAT0. A faint Grace signature only observable in sterile laboratory environments, effectively mundane. Measurements peak in the microabdel 
range. 
 
CAT1 IMP. A small Grace signature just noticeable above background abdel pressure. Able to produce or enforce minor effects similar to 
untrained exorcists. 
 
CAT2 GHEIST. A noticeable Grace signature when nearby the source. Readings can sometimes cross the abdel threshold at this tier. Can exude 
more potent effects. 
 
CAT3 REVENANT. Potency peaks at several dozen abdels. Faint visual trails can be seen by sensitive exorcists at this stage, and non sapient 
wildlife is often observed fleeing the area. 
 
CAT4 WRAITH. Grace signatures of CAT4 and higher automatically trigger a regional alarm to dispatch more specialised possibly international 
operatives. Readings in this category often reach into the hectoabdels. 
 
CAT5 DEMON. Can measure up to several kiloabdels, and can be seen as faint visual ghosting from a distance by even resistant exorcists. Grace 
fields at this stage start to leave “stains” that must be cleansed after the Irregularity is dealt with. 
 
CAT6 GOD. Grace signature potency is now noticeable by mundane humans, often described as chills or sense of unease. Grace trails are 
distinctly visual to Graced individuals at this stage, and prolonged exposure to this level of 200+ kiloabdel pressure can cause migraines, vertigo, 
and ego crises. 
 
CAT7 DEMON GOD. A Grace signature so potent it is barely visible to mundane humans. Exorcists often describe the feeling of walking through a 
CAT7 Irregularity field like a wind tunnel. Even an hour of exposure in lower CAT exorcists results in rapid onset ego death, amnesia, and 
semi-permanent brain damage. Long term mega-abdel pressure can cause widespread permanent decline in human and environmental health in 
the affected area. 
 
CAT8 RAGNAROK. It is theorized that a CAT8 Sin Event Collapse would produce a Grace signature potent enough to render earth’s surface 
uninhabitable. No recorded instances of this type of event appear in SEER History Archives. Calculations of potential readings spike abnormally 
into possible tera-abdel levels.  
 
Note: Any exorcists that display abilities beyond the expected potency of their CAT are to be immediately brought to the nearest TEMERITY 
psycholab for interrogation and investigation. 
SEER Irregularity Classification Forms: 
 
Irregularities are phenomena that defy accepted reality due to an inciting vector of high-pressure Grace thresholds. Base irregularities are 
classified by their connection to physical places and objects, aberrations by their complex corporeal manifestations, and sins by their 
anthropogenic origins and nature. You can consider each a subset of the next, similar to the familiar Squares and RecSerpents conundrum. A Sin 
is an irregularity, but not every irregularity is a sin. 
 
Note: Most Irregularities that stay within the Irregularity classification are by and large functionally inanimate objects or paranatural events rather 
than anything recognizable to the human mind as a “being”. Do not pity them. 
 
1. Irregular Manifestation that only affects itself or others via direct contact. 
1A. Irregular Manifestation that applies its effect generally over a static area. 
1B. Irregular Manifestation that applies its effect to specific targets or criteria over a static area. 
2. Irregular Abstraction that applies its effect generally over a static area. 
2A. Irregular Abstraction that applies its effect to specific targets or criteria over a static area. 
3. Irregular Abstraction that requires a vector to exert or spread its influence. 
 
+μ. Non-Anthropogenic in origin. 
+λ. Passive Irregularity. 
 


 
+Δ. Mobile Irregularity. 
+Φ. Sentient/Sapient Irregularity (Proceed to Aberration Classification). 
 
Note: Irregularities born from failed or unrealized Sin Events have shown to be drastically more useful in fabrication to TEMERITY Armament 
Studies than naturally occurring Irregularities. 
SEER Aberration Classification Forms: 
 
Aberrations can include any number of classically described “beings” composed of, native to, or otherwise tied inextricably to grace and the 
psychic sea. 
 
1. Natural in origin. 
1A. Natural in origin but manifested via anthropogenic means. 
2. Anthropogenic in origin. (Proceed to Sin Classification). 
3. Paranatural in origin. 
 
+λ. Passive Aberration. 
+Δ. Sessile Aberration. 
+Φ. Sentient/Sapient Aberration. 
+β. Parasophont Aberration. 
 
Note: Non-sin aberrations have not as of yet shown TEMERITY anything resembling humanoid intelligence, and as such are classified as 
non-sentient. Higher CAT exorcists are known to hunt low CAT Aberrations as a pastime. 
SEER Sin Classification Forms: 
 
Every sin is classified based on what person, place or event it is tethered to. Sins are innately anthropogenic and have never been observed 
forming in places completely devoid of human interaction. 
 
1. Independent of Host. 
1A. Independent of Haunt. 
2. Merged with Host. 
2A. Merged with Haunt. 
2B. Merged with Haunt and Host simultaneously . 
3. Bound by Host. 
3A. (Unobserved) Bound by Haunt. 
3B. (Unobserved) Merged with Haunt, Bound by Host or Vice Versa. 
3C. Bound by Haunt and Host simultaneously. 
4. (Singular case observed) Untethered due to extraneous circumstances. 
 
+λ. Passive Sin. Contain Only. Do not execute. 
+ΣI / ΣII. Compound Sin, multiple conjoined (I) sources or (II) manifestations. 
+Ω. Migratory Sin, can change tethers and/or Typage. 
+Φ. Sentient/Sapient Sin. 
+β. Parasophont Sin. 
 
Note: Von Hoenn Sin Theory supports the hypothesis that the psychic sea exists everywhere and that humanity simply caused it to seep, by 
proposing that Sins are the result of human suffering wearing it too thin to sustain, causing a rupture. Von Hoenn was recently detained and 
executed before CAIN High Admin for knowingly colluding with rogue binder terrorist movements to kidnap and traffick exorcists out of Castle 
grounds, and purposefully and publically aiding mundane humans with Blasphemies, in direct violation of the Pact of the Worm. 
SEER Interaction and Containment Classification: 
 
- ALEPH: Mundane handling or cooperation possible. No extensive containment procedures needed. 
 
- BET: Restraint or containment possible. Up to Schedule 4 containment procedures required. 
 
- GIMMEL: Restraint justifiable only if the subject is of equal value or can produce irregularities of equal value. Schedule 3 or higher containment 
procedures needed. 
●​
White Ring 
○​
Low risk. Easy to contain, minimal sacrifice. 
●​
Green Ring 
○​
Moderate risk. Schedule 2 or higher containment procedures needed. 
●​
Red Ring 
○​
Incredibly high risk. Requires direct approval from Prete Iherejika to contain. 
●​
Black Ring 
○​
Extreme risk. Reserved for sacrifices only ADMIN can approve. Schedule 1 containment procedures needed. 
 
- DALET: Extermination required, what they can provide cannot possibly surpass what it would cost to contain them. 
 
- SHIN: Subject is of no use or note to TEMERITY or SEER interests. Execute as normal. 
 
- TAV: Subject is to be captured or contained alive for formal execution before CAIN High Admin at Throne A1. 
 
Note: Detained Aleph class Sin hosts often reclassify as Shin class irregularities due to inability to sustain TEMERITY research interests.  
 
 
 


 
Bonus Game Rules 
 


 
TEMERITY Oddity Compendium 
Heads up these all kinda suck lmao. This is essentially my garbage bin of bonus systems and incomplete ideas. Nothing here is balanced, optimal, 
or recommended to ever use, it’s purely here for you to take and do with what you wish. It’s all stuff that didn’t make the cut. 
 
Wealth 
A new CAT row for abstracting mundane and supernatural wealth. This does not “unlock” things per CAT, but simply details examples of that price 
range.  
●​
0: Common mundane items, a meal, clothes, a basic knife or gun, practically free. 
●​
1: More pricey mundane items such as bikes, electronics, and expensive clothing. Minor supernatural items such as drugs and minor 
talismans. 
●​
2: Very expensive mundane items such as specialized equipment, vehicles, homes, and business ventures. Major supernatural items such 
as Sin Keystones, and fresh sin corpse materials. 
●​
3: Private sector information, military spec weaponry and classified mundane items, living sin trade. 
●​
4: Millionaire expenses, entire plots of land or minor companies, private mercenary squadrons. Any supernatural item of this tier or higher 
is unavailable to those not belonging to the Round Table elite. 
●​
5: Billionaire expenses, entire supercorporations and small parts of a country, information that could bring parts of society to its knees, 
nuclear weaponry.   
●​
6: Things only a handful of living people in this world have access to or can produce. 
●​
7: Things so rare there are most likely only one or two of their potencies in human history. 
 
 
 


 
House Rules 
Brilliant Flash 
Player characters gain Flash Points in the following scenarios: 
●​
Spark: 2 or more successes on a player’s roll show the same number, gaining them points equal to the amount of matching dice. If all 
matching dice are 6s, double the points gained. If this triggers two times in the same roll (such as rolling three 4s and two 5s) you only 
apply the highest of the matches (in this case the three 4s). 
●​
Conviction: a player satisfies a bolded agenda item, gaining them 1 point. If this is during a combat scene, increase it to 2 points. 
●​
Determination: a player takes 1 less stress than would inflict instant death on them, gaining them 3 points. If they take their last injury 
from this, increase it to 5 points. 
●​
The Zone: a player character successfully hits with multiple attacks in a row, gaining them 1 point per successive attack beyond the 
second. 
 
As part of a roll, a player may spend Flash Points to do the following in order: 
1.​ Before the roll, they may announce and spend 2 Flash Points to add 1 potential success to a roll, as many times as they have Flash 
Points. 
2.​ They roll the dice. 
3.​ On at least one success, add all of the purchased potential successes to the roll. 
4.​ If there are no successes, the points are still spent but do nothing. 
5.​ For every 2 Flash Points spent, they take 1 stress. 
 
These points carry between sessions and hunts, and half carry over to a player’s next character if the previous one dies or goes Imago. 
Optional: Pinpoint Flash 
A player may attempt to barter with the GM to gain and immediately expend Flash Points. A player character must be extremely focused on their 
current target, almost single mindedly. 
 
1/scene If the GM allows, a player may roll a separate 2d6 as part of an attack. If both dice are 5 or higher, the player gains CAT+1 Flash Points 
(or double if they are both 6s) which they must immediately expend. If both dice are 1s, they lose a psyche burst and take CAT sin. 
 
On any other result, the character simply takes -1D to their attack. 
Optional: Blinding Flash 
If a player character rolls double 6s on an attack they have expended Flash Points on, they add 1/2CAT more successes to the end result. 
Optional: Bloody Flash 
A player may activate divine agony on an attack to add flash points equal to double the pathos expended instead of gaining dice. Doing so 
causes 1 stress per 2 flash points as usual. 
 
Inspiration: Jujutsu Kaisen 
Balanced Scales 
With this system, a player character spends points to assign their stress cap, sin cap, injury cap, and psyche bursts. You must have at least one in 
stress and injury cap. 
 
Exorcists may start with the Default spread, or assign 42 points as they wish.  
●​
Stress Cap: 2 dot/1 cap  
●​
Injury Cap: 4 dot/1 cap  
●​
Sin Cap: 1 dot/2 cap  
●​
3 Psyche Bursts: 4 dot/1 burst  
 
Default Spread: 
●​
6 stress cap (12 dots spent) 
●​
3 injury cap(12 dots spent) 
●​
12 sin cap (6 dots spent) 
●​
3 psyche bursts (12 dots spent) 
 
Extreme Example Spreads: 
●​
Meathead: 
○​
8 stress cap (16 dots spent) 
○​
6 injury cap (24 dots spent) 
○​
2 sin cap (1 dot spent) 
○​
0 psyche burst (0 dots spent) 
●​
Hubris: 
○​
2 stress cap (1 dots spent) 
○​
1 injury cap (4 dots spent) 
○​
0 sin cap (0 dots spent) 
○​
9 psyche bursts (36 dots spent) 
 
 
 


 
Moderate Example Spreads: 
●​
Stocky: 
○​
7 stress cap (14 dots spent) 
○​
4 injury cap (16 dots spent) 
○​
8 sin cap (4 dots spent) 
○​
2 psyche bursts (8 dots spent) 
●​
Attuned: 
○​
6 stress cap (12 dots spent) 
○​
2 injury cap (4 dots spent) 
○​
12 sin cap (6 dots spent) 
○​
4 psyche bursts (16 dots spent) 
Optional: Weighted Scales 
If a player chooses to give themselves a certain stat spread, they may gain a corresponding benefit. They can only benefit from one caste at once: 
●​
Cursed Caste: 2 or less injury cap, 4 or less stress cap 
○​
Gain 1 more psyche burst slot.  
○​
Cast blasphemies at +2 CAT, CAT times per mission.  
○​
You may expend a psyche burst or take 1d3 sin to nullify a non-severe attack or source of stress/injury against you. 
○​
You regain 2 psyche bursts for free whenever pressure increases.  
○​
You cannot roll your own dice to regain stress during a rest. 
○​
+1D during PSYCHE clash. 
●​
Null Caste: 1 or less psyche bursts, 2 or less sin cap 
○​
Your mundane physical capabilities extend to ½ CAT (rounded down). 
○​
CAT/scene you may move in a straight line up to a short distance nearly instantly as if you teleported, purely from physical 
speed.  
○​
You may activate a High Blasphemy by taking a single injury instead of spending psyche bursts.  
○​
You regain 1d3 stress for free whenever pressure increases. 
○​
All mundane weapons deal half stress to you rounded down. 
○​
Sin overflow does not reduce your cap. If you overflow with 6 or more sin marks, you cannot Resist. 
○​
Cannot PSYCHE clash. 
●​
Exorcist Caste: Close to average stat spread 
○​
Normal exorcist abilities. 
○​
Choose a sin mark from a random location for free. 
Optional: Measured Scales 
A player character may spend an advancement during downtime to give themselves 2 dots to spend. These can be saved until they wish to spend 
them. 
Optional: Tipping Scales 
A player character may reallocate up to CATx2 dots between their stats during downtime by spending 2 scrip. 
Stricture of Worms 
The player character may attempt to bargain with the GM. In exchange for restricting an action of the GM’s choice to 0d or 1d (which they must tell 
you), similar to the Strictures of virtues, you may do one of the following things once: 
 
●​
Resist: Defy Fate without needing to sin overflow. 
●​
Ascend: Gain the benefits of sin overflow without lowering your sin cap or gaining a sin mark. 
●​
Smite: Flip a coin. On heads, instantly reduce a talisman of your choice by half its maximum, including hooks (which can erase them), 
event or process clocks (which automatically completes them) or up to CAT enemy talismans. 
 
It is recommended the restricted action be incredibly difficult for the character, in exchange for this power. Strictures enacted this way are 
permanent and cannot be removed, though you can take as many as you want. You can ignore a stricture for one roll by taking 1d3 nonlethal 
stress. For every 2 strictures you take, your stress cap reduces by 1. 
 
It is recommended this ability be saved for very high level or powerful characters, including NPCs as the GM sees fit (try not to be an asshole 
about it, please GMs). 
Optional: Stricture of Vermin 
An exorcist may make a Stricture of Worms to forfeit a portion of their lifespan in exchange for incredible power. They may bargain with the GM, 
who lets them choose one of the following effects based on what they feel is appropriate for the amount of time sacrificed: 
 
●​
Resist: Regain stress and/or injuries. 
●​
Ascend: The next power you cast is treated as a higher CAT, and can have additional effects as determined by the GM for its extreme 
nature. 
●​
Smite: Instantly kill or maim combatant you can see in a cinematic way. 
Optional: Stricture of Pests 
If an exorcist is at the brink of death, the player may bargain with the GM to forfeit a large chunk of their lifespan to gain one of the following 
effects: 
●​
Persist: Return to full stress and injury cap. You are effectively invincible for the next CAT rounds. Gain a sin mark of your choice, which 
does not affect your sin cap. Black Blood cannot revive you anymore. 
●​
Descend: Go Imago for the scene, gaining the benefits of a sin overflow and increasing your CAT by 1. Attacking supernatural entities 
with your new natural weapons is no longer hard for you for the scene. Black Blood cannot revive you anymore. 
●​
Spite: Tie your threads of fate together. If you die, whoever you linked to you will be dragged screaming into oblivion, no matter what. This 
is permanent, and persists even if you end up not dying. Black Blood cannot revive you anymore. 
 


 
Optional: Stricture of Parasites 
An exorcist may make a binding vow, a promise to their own soul in exchange for more power. The player may bargain with the GM to generate a 
new stricture that follows the rules of previous strictures, such as: 
●​
Reducing a course of action to 1 or 0d 
●​
Making a course of action unchangeably hard or risky 
●​
Making a course of action unchangeably impossible 
●​
Dealing stress when you attempt to do something 
●​
Inflicting death when you attempt to do something 
●​
Another mechanical or narrative effect the GM and player agree on 
In exchange they may bargain with the GM to gain a permanent bonus to a specific course of action such as: 
●​
Reducing difficulty by a step 
●​
+1 or +2d bonus 
●​
Automatic successes 
●​
Raising the CAT of abilities 
●​
Reducing stress from a certain source 
●​
Increasing slashes dealt to a certain target 
●​
Another narrative effect both parties feel is appropriate. 
 
The restriction should be enforceable, such as only using your powers on members of a certain organization and rolling 0d when attacking anyone 
but them, but in exchange you gain +2d when attacking them, and cast powers against them at +1 CAT. 
 
Inspiration: Jujutsu Kaisen, Hunter x Hunter 
Optional: Stricture of ██████ 
If an exorcist is on the brink of death and a Sin is about to make a reaction, the GM may make an offer. In exchange for saving or sparing their 
life, the exorcist must absorb the Sin as a Type 2.5 Sin, half fused and half bound.  
 
The hunt ends as if the group had spared the Sin. The exorcist appears entirely normal to the rest of the party and CAIN Admin, but cutting them 
open or making them bleed reveals that the entire interior of their body has been replaced by paranaturally regenerating sin-flesh, like a bag of 
gelatin wearing human skin.  
 
The exorcist must never reveal their true nature or else they will be immediately brought to THRONE-1A for execution. This threat should be 
constantly looming over the character during downtime. However, a bound exorcist never needs to pay scrip/get procedures done to repair 
grievous wounds or lost limbs, as they have no actual internal skeleton or organs. It simply regenerates by the time they return to dorms.  
 
The exorcist gains a constant inner voice that represents their new parasite, and can often have visual hallucinations of it appearing near them 
when conversing to it, like a more present and talkative Shadow. Obviously, the level of conversation varies depending on the nature of the sin 
itself. 
 
1/mission the GM may have the bound sin make a demand appropriate to the sin’s personality, regarding a current circumstance or event. The 
exorcist flips 1d2. On a success, they may choose to take an injury to resist the demand. On a failure, they are compelled to follow the demand. 
This action usually will not be to directly sabotage the exorcist, as their survival means the sin’s survival, and discovery means execution. This is 
simply a means of putting the exorcist into courses of action that would directly oppose or dig deeper into the exorcist’s beliefs. 
 
The host exorcist immediately gains the following ability: 
 
Demon Trigger 
 
Until Rest, Transmute, Self, ½CAT/mission 
 
As an action, you loosen the chain of flesh holding in your sinful nature, spending a psyche burst and taking 1d3-1 sin. You immediately manifest 
the following effects based on how many times you have used this ability in the same mission: 
●​
1-2: One limb or feature of your choice manifests into a mundane ½ CAT replica of your bound sin. If you hit with an attack with this 
feature, the next attack against the target cannot be hard, as you impact, tear into, shock, or pin down the target in some way. 
a.​ You may suppress and manifest the visual effects of this for free at any time as an action for the duration. 
●​
3-4: Your head and 3 other limbs or features morph into a mundane CAT replica of your bound sin. Immediately erase 1d2-1 injury 
slashes, and reduce all stress taken for the rest of the scene by 1. All mundane attacks against you are hard for the duration.  
○​
Roll 1d6. On a 1, the Sin takes over, and you become an Imago that is loosely but still partially tied to the party. You regain control 
after the duration ends. 
○​
You permanently look as if tier 1 of this ability was active for the rest of the hunt. You can take 1d3 stress to suppress the visual 
effects for a scene, and the effect goes away at the end of the hunt. 
●​
5+: Your entire body ruptures and releases the bound sin for a scene.  
○​
Roll 1d3. On a 1, the Sin takes over, and you become an Imago that is barely but still partially tied to the party, and can be 
persuaded to betray them if it aligns with the sin’s interests. You regain control after the duration ends. 
○​
You permanently look as if tier 2 of this ability was active for the rest of the hunt. You can take 1d3 stress to suppress the visual 
effects for a scene, and the effect goes away at the end of the hunt. 
 
Notes: 
●​
You may activate this power multiple times in the same scene as an action to speed through the tiers. 
●​
At higher tiers, you gain the benefits UP TO that tier. So mechanically at tier 3 you would be receiving the benefits of tiers 1, 2, and 3. 
●​
This optional rule should never be used without agreement from the entire table.  
●​
Some domains might need more improv-related effects if they rely more on time passing, such as the faster infection domain of a 
Centipede (simply grant them a small horde of ally zombies). 
●​
The PLAYER acts as the sin in this case. They retain control of their character technically, but now must try to operate them from the 
perspective of the entity that is controlling them, with pointers from the GM. 
●​
This isnt balanced at all lmfao. I made this in a 4 AM daze and played a one shot with my friends and we had a blast but no clue what it 
means for long term campaigns. 
 
Inspiration: Kaiju No. 8, Chainsaw Man 
 
 


 
Soul Signature 
You may choose powers from any blasphemy, incorporating them into your own unique sinseed by not gaining the passive. If a power requires the 
passive to function, you are considered to have the passive for the instant you use the power.  
 
However, once you reach 3 powers total and want more, or want the benefit of another passive at all times, you must take the sin cap reduction as 
normal. Whenever you gain a new passive, you must take a power from that blasphemy. 
 
Every time you take a new passive, the amount of power “slots” you unlock reduces by one (second passive is three powers, third is two powers, 
fourth and beyond is one power), and the sin cap cost increases (second and third passive is 1 sin cap, fourth and beyond is 2 sin cap). 
 
During character creation, if the character chooses two powers from separate blasphemies, they may only gain the passive from one of those 
blasphemies (their choice). 
 
Mimic Technique from the sorcerer agenda is changed to the following when using this rule: 
●​
Mimic Technique 
At the start of every other hunt, choose one blasphemy power. You do not gain its passive unless it is necessary for the power to function, 
in which case you are considered to have the passive for the instant you use the power. You may treat the ability as one of your own until 
you choose a new one. 
Metastisize 
The unstable, violent natures of Sins are not the only manifestations of human suffering. There is also the insidious, seeping, staining flow of 
constant grudges and emotion, the endless tide of not-quite-catastrophic ill will. From these, are born not Sins, but Malignants. 
 
Malignants are somewhat stable “reocurring” villains, who aren’t subject to the same Sin Event cycles and unraveling nature of the most violent 
Sins. They are never quite as overtly powerful, but have the capability for higher intelligence and benefit from feasting on the ambient suffering of 
their domain rather than a single host’s suffering. Their amount of misanthropy varies, from feeling “slighted” or having savior complexes, to being 
genocidal human-hating forces of destruction. Whether they work with Binders also varies from case to case 
 
Malignants are made of raw sinflesh, almost a living human-sized Sin Seed. They feast on psychic energy to survive. CAIN is wholly unaware of 
their existence given their ability to control themselves (to some degree) and often dispatches exorcists to fight them thinking they are lower-level 
sins due to the lower grace emissions. 
 
Malignants differ from Sins in the following ways: 
●​
CAT Scaling: 
○​
Malignants do not experience Sin Events the same way sins do, instead scaling alongside the players as they progress.  
■​
If you create them at a higher CAT than the exorcists to start out with as a sort of Super Villain, they should not increase in 
CAT until the players match their current CAT.  
○​
Malignant powers and Domains scale with CAT-1 (minimum of CAT1) 
●​
Domains: 
○​
They possess only 2 Domains from their “original sin” so to speak, and also possess warped versions of a Blasphemy power or 
two related to their personality and domain. 
■​
A Malignant’s Blasphemy powers scale with their CAT as usual, but are often bizarre, malformed, and sometimes entirely 
unique to their individual being. It uses these powers as a reaction (attack, threat or complication) as usual. 
■​
A Malignant can manifest and demanifest their domains at will most of the time, as they have greater control over their 
soul and their powers.  
■​
You do not have to choose 2 domains from the same Sin, and can mix and match as appropriate. The same goes for 
Blasphemy powers. These domains are slightly weaker, more localized, and are more like blasphemies than 
investigation-area spanning effects. 
●​
Execution: 
○​
Malignants’ talismans do not factor in Pressure and instead simply count as [3-5]+CAT depending on how bulky you think they 
should be. They also simply “retreat” from the hunt when they are defeated, given their “villain of the week” nature.  
■​
During moments of extreme narrative culmination (or just if it would be Really Fucking Cool) the players can officially kill 
them, but otherwise they should simply fall back and continue plotting. 
■​
Malignants should be barely present in the first 1-2 hunts, then more so afterwards as the players discover whatever plot 
or meddling they have been engaging in. This can range from simply causing suffering to a long and spiderwebbed plan of 
humanity’s annihilation. 
●​
Group: 
○​
When using a small squad or group of Malignants, they have a shared execution talisman. 
■​
Talisman size is equal to: 
●​
Add together all base talismans before CAT from the members. 
●​
Divide by the number of members, rounded up. 
●​
Add the number of members, plus the highest CAT among them. 
■​
Roughly every ⅓ of the talisman, a member of the group is effectively “taken out” and cannot take reactions. 
●​
Attacks: 
○​
(1) 3-4 stress, (2/3): 2-3 stress, (4+): 1-2 stress. 
■​
They should deal less stress than actual sins directly overall, unless they are built or designed to be tanky or glass cannon 
melee fighters.  
■​
Using threats and making complications with their powers to increase stress dealt should be common. They are crafty, 
clever, and usually tend to use their powers and environment to their advantage, even if they are more violent Sins like 
Malignant Hounds. 
■​
Malignants can engage in PSY CLASH much like Binders. 
●​
Nature: 
○​
Malignants are not formed from a single host, but from the burbling cauldron of human emotion in a wide area. 
■​
A Malignant Centipede for example would not wish to cause suffering for a specific host, but rather applies their 
misanthropy to humanity at large. 
■​
Some malignants will even travel the world cannibalizing other Malignants of a similar domain to feed from their populace 
as well.  
 


 
■​
The most powerful Malignants are usually related to common fears of the time, such as War and Politics, AI and 
Technology, Famine and Plague, Jealousy and Social Media, Racism and Bigotry, Natural Disasters, Climate Change, et 
cetera. 
○​
Despite their controlled nature, they are not immortal and will eventually unravel into a Sin proper either with time, stress, or as a 
last ditch effort. 
 
Reworks 
Sin Mark Rework 
Mark: Boon 
Permanent benefits, evolutions towards a graced apotheosis. Basically just roll here instead of on the normal table if you want all sin marks to be 
radical. 
 
1: Eyes/Ears 
1.​ You gain the ability to see through nonliving matter up to ½ CAT distance. 
2.​ You gain the ability to see either Infrared or UV light within a long distance, which you cannot change once chosen. 
3.​ You gain the ability to hear clearly up to long range, as long as there is open space between you and a target you can see. 
4.​ Your eyes gain the ability to zoom in, seeing up to CAT+1 distance as if it was adjacent to you. 
5.​ ½ CAT/mission you may lock eyes with a human, freezing them solid in place for up to 2 minutes if you maintain eye contact. 
6.​ You gain the ability to understand all spoken dialects, even nonhuman ones. 
 
2: Mouth/Nose 
1.​ If you have analyzed a creature in a combat scene, you may track them easily up to an extreme distance by trailing their unique grace 
signature. 
2.​ Your jaws become a lethal weapon able to bite through steel and stone, and you may upgrade them with scrip like a service weapon. 
3.​ You are entirely immune to most bloodstream and ingested toxins and poisons, and can digest any matter regardless of sharpness or 
material. 
4.​ Your tongue becomes a fifth limb, which you are able to manipulate with some dexterity and is sticky enough to grab things. 
5.​ You can whisper short 5-word messages into the air as an action, targeting one creature within CAT+1 distance regardless of obstructions, 
which hears it at the same volume. 
6.​ ½ CAT/mission you may issue a single-word command to any human within short range, who will immediately and unquestioningly obey it 
as long as it is not obviously harmful. 
 
3: Chest/Back 
1.​ You no longer need to breathe, and are entirely immune to inhaled toxins and clouds of particulates. 
2.​ Gain +2 kp, and the ability to lift 5x the weight the strongest human could. 
3.​ 1/rest when gaining an injury, roll a d6. On a 6, ignore the injury and remain at 0 stress. This does not expend if you do not roll a 6. 
4.​ A lost limb or major wound fully recovers at the end of the next mission. Automatically erase 1 stress when pressure increases. 
5.​ Whenever you take an injury, the next source of stress deals 1d3 less stress but you gain 1 sin. 
6.​ 1/scene you may deal PSYCHE slashes to an enemy that deals stress to you with a melee attack, without rolling. 
 
4: Legs/Arms 
1.​ Once per mission an object of your choice melds with your body. It does not cost KP to pull out, and may be summoned directly from your 
arm or leg. 
2.​ Once a mission, you may morph one limb of your choice into a supernatural weapon for one scene by taking 1d3+2 sin. Attacking sins 
with this weapon is not hard. 
3.​ Your mundane reach increases to short distance, and you can reach through areas down to 3 inches in diameter. 
4.​ You gain the ability to spider-climb on a solid surface in any direction as long as your flesh is touching it. 
5.​ 1/rest you may run in a straight line up to CAT distance instantly, as if teleporting, as an action. 
6.​ You may take up to CATx2 steps in the air as if on invisible platforms, but cannot stand still in the air. This refreshes when you rest. 
 
5: Skin/Bones 
1.​ Once per mission, you may take 1d3 sin to instantly melt up to CAT cubic meters of matter into a black sludge by making contact with your 
full palm and fingers. 
2.​ Your skin becomes as tough as diamond. 1/scene you may take -1 stress from a mundane attack against you. 
3.​ You gain a prehensile tail strong enough to hang from. Gain +1D on rolls made to balance. 
4.​ You may take 1d3 sin to collapse inwards, able to shrink small enough to fit anywhere the diameter of your face can fit. 
5.​ You may take 1d3 sin to turn completely invisible for the duration of a single action. 
6.​ You gain the ability to emit an extremely fast acting poison from your skin. You may make a mundane attack against any being that 
touches your bare skin. 
 
6: Mutation (1d3) 
1.​ Roll for location, choose an ability. 
2.​ Choose a location, roll for its ability. 
3.​ Choose a location, choose an ability. 
 
 


 
Mark: Bane 
Permanent scars, wounds on your soul and body. Basically just roll here instead of on the normal table if you want all sin marks to suck. 
 
1: Eyes/Ears 
1.​ You go completely blind, but see blurry images of living things within short range as warped and terrifying shapes. 
2.​ You lose the ability to read all writing except things you have written or typed yourself. 
3.​ You go completely deaf, but can hear the darkest thoughts of living things within short range as barely audible whispers. 
4.​ Whenever anyone (including yourself) looks at your uncovered eyes directly you both take 1d3 stress. 
5.​ 1/scene you audibly hear terrible insults or threats in the voice of one person near you. 
6.​ You gain a violent sensitivity to one sense (roll 1d2): (1) sight, (2) sound. Whenever you are in the presence of a strong or intense source 
of that sense you roll -1D on all rolls unless you cover or protect yourself. 
 
2: Mouth/Nose 
1.​ Whenever you are near a source of blood or a person that is bleeding severely, you roll -1D on all rolls that do not inflict violence on the 
bleeding victim. If you do not act on this impulse by the end of the scene, take 1 sin. 
2.​ A major feature of your face or mouth warps into a grotesque and terrifying visage. All rolls made to socialize with others are risky if you 
are not covered. 
3.​ 1/rest when you are speaking, the GM can roll a d6. On a 3 or lower, you take 1d3 stress as you bite into your mouth or tongue with 
warped teeth.  
4.​ You completely lose the ability to speak out loud, or your words are incomprehensible. You can still sign, write, and think like normal. 
5.​ Whenever you fail a roll to speak to or socialize with others, your mouth slips in a cutting insult or aggravating comment. 
6.​ You gain a violent sensitivity to one sense (roll 1d2): (1) smell, (2) taste. Whenever you are in the presence of a strong or intense source of 
that sense you roll -1D on all rolls unless you cover or protect yourself. 
 
3: Chest/Back 
1.​ Your breathing becomes incredibly loud and wheezing, making actions that require silence or stealth hard by default. 
2.​ You can carry -1 maximum KP, and you have a hard time lifting anything heavier than 10 lbs. 
3.​ 1/mission when you gain an injury, the GM can increase the cap reduction by 1, which bypasses other effects. 
4.​ You roll 1 less rest die during rests unless you sleep in an area of extreme comfort or luxury. 
5.​ Whenever you gain sin, the next roll that requires significant physical exertion or stamina becomes hard. 
6.​ 1/mission when afflicted by a poison or dense cloud of gas/particles, your senses and thoughts become blurred making using them hard 
for a scene. 
 
4: Legs/Arms 
1.​ Your gait becomes lurching and unsteady. Balance becomes difficult, and your walking is incredibly conspicuous to onlookers. 
2.​ Your hands are cursed. Any handheld mundane item you touch with your bare hands breaks irreparably after a single action. This does 
not apply to objects you wish to destroy on purpose. 
3.​ Your flesh becomes less resilient. Whenever you take 6 or more stress in a single instance, an appendage of your choice is mauled or 
ripped off. 
4.​ Your hands gain a mind of their own. 1/rest as a complication the GM may describe you dropping, throwing, or otherwise interfering with 
an item you are holding or can touch. Roll PSYCHE to resist, taking 1d3 sin and stopping the interference on a success. 
5.​ You seem to slip and trip on everything as if oiled. 1/scene when you fail a roll to balance, jump, or move quickly the GM may roll a risk die 
after the action has been completed. On a 1, something terrible happens as you slip, trip, overshoot or fall over. 
6.​ 1/scene when you roll no successes on an attack, if the risk die is 2 or lower the GM may direct your attack to an ally in short distance of 
the original target, making you roll again. 
 
5: Skin/Bones 
1.​ Being able to see your own skin causes you to roll -1D on all rolls until you are covered. 
2.​ Your skin or bones become transmuted and stiff, making moving at high speeds or reacting quickly hard unless you take 1d3 stress. 
3.​ Being near or touching others becomes physically painful for you. Whenever you participate in set up or teamwork that requires physical 
touch, aid in a severe attack, or physically touch another person within adjacent range you take 1d2 sin and pain wracks your nerves. 
4.​ You have a gaping wound in your skin that never seems to stop bleeding or seeping fluid. Getting hit by an attack aimed at your wound 
inflicts +1 stress on a hit. 
5.​ You grow painful growths all over your body (1d2): (1) eyes, (2) mouths. Covering them reduces rolls made with their relevant ability (sight 
or speech) hard, but humans will immediately recognize you as inhuman if you do not cover them. 
6.​ Your skin rots away when not maintained. Every time pressure increases without a rest, take 1 stress and your next roll made with 
physical skills rolls -1D. 
 
6: Curse. You may choose this option and forgo rolling for a location if you wish. 
1.​ Your average life expectancy (not including any outside threats) is drastically reduced. Your expected time left before suffering 
unavoidable death is no more than a year.  
2.​ When you suffer instant death and fail to or choose not to Defy Fate, you instantly solidify into a solid statue made from a material of your 
choice. You cannot be revived by Black Blood medicine. 
3.​ Everyone, including your allies and friends, will immediately forget everything about your existence and presence if you die. Records will 
posthumously warp to account for your retroactive absence. 
4.​ Roll for a mark location, choose a scar. 
5.​ Choose a mark location, roll for a scar. 
6.​ Choose a mark location, choose a scar. 
Mark: Atrophy 
When you gain a sin mark, do the following: 
-​
Roll for a location for your Boon mark, then roll for its ability (or choose if your GM permits it.) 
-​
Roll for a different location for your Bane mark, then roll its ability. 
 
Whenever you gain another sin mark, you may choose to add another Boon and/or Bane in the same locations as the first, atrophying one part of 
your body in order to further another. If you choose to roll for a Bane’s location, you may choose the Bane if it lands in the same location as a Boon 
you have. You still take the lowered sin cap regardless of if you use the same location or roll a new one. 
 
You may spend advancements to add abilities to your sin mark as normal. 
 


 
Heretic Campaign 
 


 
HERETIC CAMPAIGN RULES 
 
There are those who stand against CAIN. Good or bad, oppressive or liberating, they all claim between themselves the moniker of 
UNDER//HEAVEN, sporting the arrow-tipped sigil of a corrupt paradise. Players may either defect from CAIN, gaining entry to the ranks of 
UNDER//HEAVEN, or be found by them before CAIN has a chance to shackle them down. Regardless, you are now unburdened by the dogma of 
an organization who sees you as subhuman. You now belong to those who see you as so much more. Get loud. Get messy.  
 
PUSH AGAINST THE GRAIN 
 
Playing as a Heretic rather than an Exorcist comes with quite a few modifications to the base ruleset of CAIN. The first and foremost is that the 
corporate hierarchy that divides the ranks of CAIN and their tools does not exist even in the slightest amongst the disparate legions of 
UNDER//HEAVEN. Players are free to sleep where they want, eat what they want, wear what they want, take what they want. So long as no one 
else tries to kill them over it. 
 
As a result, all players receive the following effects: 
●​
Players are considered to possess any aesthetics kit expansion they please. They can buy, borrow, or steal fashion as they wish, and can 
get wild with their appearance. 
●​
Scrip is replaced by Favours, which function almost identically to scrip aside from the bonus ability to Cash In A Favour during downtime 
(detailed further on). Likewise, mentions of CAIN are replaced by their Clan name. 
●​
Players are allowed to keep objects they find on hunts, up to CAT+1 objects, which they can store in their living quarters or carry with them 
as they travel. 
●​
Players are not restricted in their ability to move or explore during downtime. If they can steal, sneak, or travel somewhere while avoiding 
the radar of CAIN, the whole wide world is open to them. Almost all major cities have a dedicated U//H underground, and most countries 
have a dedicated U//H bunker or tunnel system. 
●​
A player group is called a Coven, and most Covens don’t cooperate with one another. 
●​
All heretics have 1 extra injury and psyche burst cap. 
 
EVERY SINNER FOR THEMSELVES 
 
Hunt Bidding is a large and complex process set up by the Octagram to divy up missions and hunts between the vast swatches of heretics across 
the world. Lower-priority hunts are always available, but the big fish always have big bids.  
 
There are five main tiers of hunts: 
●​
White Tag, any CAT 
○​
Grants no reputation 
○​
Always available without having to bid. 
○​
Grants Favours as normal like any other hunt. 
●​
Green Tag, CAT 2+ only 
○​
Grants 1 rep and 1 bonus Favours on successful binding. 
○​
1 Favour buy-in 
●​
Red Tag, CAT 3+ only 
○​
Grants 2 rep and 2 bonus Favours on successful binding. 
○​
1 Favour buy-in 
●​
Violet Tag, CAT 4+ only 
○​
Grants 3 rep and 3 bonus Favours on successful binding. 
○​
2 Favour buy-in 
●​
Black Tag, CAT 5 only 
○​
Grants 5 rep and 5 bonus Favours on successful binding. 
○​
3 favour buy-in 
 
A Hunt Bid works in the following way 
●​
Heretics are aware of the number of Covens in the bid ahead of time. All hunt bids are Green Tag and higher. 
●​
Heretics pay the buy-in fee, which is spent regardless of if they win or not.  
●​
Heretics and the GM both roll 1d6. Whoever gets the highest roll gets to set the initial bid. 
●​
After the players decide on a bid, the GM rolls a risk die for each Coven in the bid: 
○​
1: The coven adds 2 to the current bid. 
○​
2-5: The coven adds 1 to the current bid. 
○​
6: The coven drops out. 
●​
Usually, Covens will stop bidding or start getting dissuaded if the bid is higher than they think it is worth. The GM can drop out a coven at 
any time they feel is appropriate. Most covens don’t have bottomless funds to bid with. 
●​
If the heretics win the bid, they pay ½ the total bid in favours (the other half is considered hazard pay) and gain the right to be the Hunters 
for that hunt. 
●​
If the heretics lose the bid, they are granted a White Tag hunt instead. 
  
If any other Coven intrudes on the heretic’s hunt, they have the right to report them to the Octagram for punishment and infringement on their turf.  
 
 


 
US AGAINST THE WORLD 
 
The target of Hunts does not change. Your objective is still to contact and subdue a Sin. But, instead of Execution, your goal is to now Bind the 
sin. If the host is dead, it is your job to find a suitable host, or take it on yourself. You do not have a Handler who handles the briefing and 
management of the case, instead it is the heretic’s job to be familiar with the potential asset they are tracking during downtime. You still learn the 
sin type and its trauma questions as usual, but also learn whether the manifestation is active or postmortem. This information is presented in a 
way as if the heretics had collected or scouted that information themselves. Generally, heretics do not receive supervision for their hunts, and are 
not subject to the arcane bylaws of CAIN investigation. 
THE COUNTDOWN 
The pressure clock now no longer exists. Instead, you gain a 6-section Countdown clock that slashes when tension fills, representing the time 
before which Exorcists arrive at the scene. After exorcists arrive, it is a race against the clock before they execute or spare the sin before you can 
get to it. This can represent them arriving at the same time, and simply working around them, or racing before they arrive at the scene in the first 
place. Whether these exorcists are overtly hostile, or potentially new allies to bring into the folds of UNDER//HEAVEN is entirely up to the GM. At 
Countdown 4+, the exorcists are now actively present in their mission and the heretics have two more ticks before the sin destabilizes and CAIN 
orders bombardment or virtue deployment for a mission failure. 
 
Exorcist 
 
The hounds of CAIN. The loyal lapdogs and mindless drones of an organization better suited to stabbing than suturing. 
Either wipe 'em out, or bring' em in. Just make sure they don’t mess with our work. 
 
Exorcists (inhumans) Execution talisman 2+(CAT) solo, 4+(CAT) group, 6+(CAT) squadron. 
 
●​ Armed with blasphemies and their ADMIN-approved service weapons. 
 
Reactions: 
Inflict Stress: Attacks with: blades, guns, whips, unique blasphemy powers. (1): 3 stress, (2/3): 2 stress, (4+): 1 stress. 
 
Or: create a complication or threat: stagger a heretic, initiate teamwork, cover their tracks, duck behind cover, use a 
Blasphemy. 
 
Or: Use a Blasphemy (1/2): Threat: 3/mission the exorcist may use a special power related to their Blasphemy powers. 
These are very restrictive, and do not have the same freeform use as heretic’s Blasphemies. The blasphemy can deal 
stress, change the terrain, or change the narrative however the GM deems appropriate. 
 
THE CONFRONTATION 
Binding a sin is a lengthy process. Successfully binding a sin requires you to exploit its trauma questions rather than answer them, which deals 
stress as normal but also permanently reduces the sin’s talisman and restores the Countdown timer by one each time you use one, chipping 
away at its grace signature before it can register on CAIN SEER installations. Once the Countdown reaches 4+, it can no longer be restored. 
Heretics must exploit two or more trauma questions in order to attempt to Bind the sin. 
 
Once the Sin’s execution talisman has been slashed completely, and two or more trauma questions have been exploited, the heretics gain the 
ability to bind the sin. 
●​
If the host is alive, it is bound to the host, and you bring them with you to become a new member of U//H. The coven automatically gains a 
strength 1 bond with the new member, if possible. 
●​
If the host is dead, the heretics must have found a suitable host (such as another NPC with a similar disposition to the sin, or someone 
related to them) or Keystone, which is gained upon exploiting all three trauma questions. A Keystone is simply an object with immense 
resonance with the sin that can contain it long enough to bring back to your faction’s headquarters for proper binding by a member. 
 
or…. 
●​
If a player is willing, they may instead bind the sin to themselves as a substitute host. Each player can only have one bound sin at any 
given time, and must have the Keystone for that sin in order to store it and swap it out for another one. The bonuses for bound sins will be 
detailed later on. The coven gains 1 extra Reputation. 
 
Succeeding the hunt grants 1 Reputation to the coven, which may be cashed in during downtime for various bonuses. 
 
Failing to bind a sin before the Countdown reaches 6 results in a hunt failure, and each heretic loses Favours equal to the CAT of the sin. Given 
how important Favours are for finding new hunts, this can easily knock their potential hunt priority down a notch. If any NPC got you an in on the 
job, or vouched for you, the strength of any bond a heretic has with them is reduced by 1. 
 
 
 
 


 
KICK BACK AND RELAX 
 
A heretic’s downtime is much more meaningful than an exorcist's. Due to their relative freedom, it is up to them to scout out potential hunts, vie for 
dominance in an area, and take on missions to defend their Reputation. Every exorcist has a Playbook given to them by their faction, which they 
can use to procure new hunts, bonuses for hunts, and improvements to their Home Base. Exorcists gain two Downtime Tokens per session, which 
they may spend on the following downtime options: 
MAKE A DEAL 
A heretic attempts to trade, barter, or haggle for an item of their choice beyond normally buying them with favors (which would not cost a 
downtime token). Seeking a particularly rare or expensive item may incur spending a token. For each Downtime Token spent they may choose one 
of the following options: 
●​
Expend a bond to reduce the price in favors by its strength. A 3 strength bond can be expended to cover 5 favors worth of cost. 
●​
Spend a point of Reputation to reduce the price by 2. 
●​
Barter an item of value to lower or match the price. 
●​
Take on a 4-slash Debt talisman that slashes every time they complete or fail a hunt, or gain Reputation. If the hook fills before they pay it 
back, a group of repossession goons will come after them and they lose 1 Reputation. 
MAKE A CONNECTION 
All NPCs the heretics wish to bond with have a 4 slash bond talisman and a strength of 0 to start. A player can only have 3 of these bonds at once, 
and the max strength of a bond is 3. The heretic plays the social web, testing and strengthening connections new and old. They roll a relevant skill 
of their choice, marking slashes on one bond talisman of their choice equal to the number of successes. If this fills the bond talisman, raise the 
strength of that bond by 1. 
 
A heretic may expend a Bond to add bonus dice equal to its strength, or spend a point of Reputation to automatically add 2 successes. 
MAKE A MOVE 
The heretic gathers information about potential hunts and sins. They roll a relevant skill of their choice, gaining Favours equal to the amount of 
successes rolled, which can only be spent on bidding for hunts. Rolling no successes leads the heretic on a false trail, deducting 1 favour and 
wasting the downtime action. Additionally, if 3 or more dice are rolled and they are all successes, the heretics learn a distinct clue towards the tie 
of a major npc in the investigation at the beginning of the next hunt. This can only trigger once, and refreshes when the next hunt ends. 
 
A heretic may expend a bond to add bonus dice equal to its strength, or spend a point of Reputation to automatically add 2 successes. 
MAKE A MEMORY 
The heretic spends time performing a leisure activity of their choice. They roll a relevant skill of their choice, gaining Favours equal to the amount 
of successes rolled, which can only be spent on casual items and housing options. Rolling no successes causes a complication to occur, which the 
GM rolls a risk die for. On a 1, a confrontation or actual conflict happens as something is misunderstood or an accident happens between a heretic 
of a rival clan. 
 
Regardless of the outcome, all heretics involved start the next hunt with +1 stress and sin cap, These bonuses disappear when the hunt ends. 
 
A heretic may expend a bond to add bonus dice equal to its strength, or spend a point of Reputation to automatically add 2 successes. 
MAKE A PROJECT 
The heretic spends time creating or maintaining a project or creation of their choice. This can include building things, tinkering with objects, 
performing rituals, and other means of creating things. They negotiate a price with the GM based on the nature of their project and its feasibility 
within the setting (look to the Black Market and scrip expansions from the core book for ideas of things exorcists might want to brew or make).  
 
The project then gains a talisman with 4-8 slashes, depending on how difficult it would be to complete or manufacture the project. The heretic then 
rolls a relevant skill, adding successes to the talisman equal to the number of successes. Rolling zero success introduces a failure or complication 
into the project, forcing the heretic to pay favors equal to half of its talisman, or reduce it by one slash. If this would reduce it below zero slashes, 
the project completely fails and must be started over. 
 
A heretic may expend a bond to add bonus dice equal to its strength minus 1, or spend a point of Reputation or relevant materials of an extreme 
cost to automatically mark 1 slash on its talisman. 
MAKE A STAND 
The heretic challenges a member of a rival Coven. This initiates a conflict scene with one rival member of their choice, who gains the Binder 
statblock and an appropriate CAT. A large, open arena is chosen by a third party. An exorcist may Play Dirty and spend a point of Reputation to 
choose the arena, or rig traps that they can trigger to hinder the binder. If the heretic wins, they gain 1 Rep. If the heretic loses, they lose 1 Rep 
and suffer instant death.
 
 


 
GOT MINE, FUCK YOU 
 
…However 
Rules are more of a suggestion than a… rule. Per se. There’s only so much to go around beyond the peripherals of CAIN, only scraps of scraps. 
The more you get comfortable with taking, the easier you’ll have it. 
PHANTOM THIEVES 
Heretics may commit a transgression against the Octagram to infringe on a hunt they lost. The hunt starts like normal but a rival Coven is out 
and about attempting to bind the sin at the same time. The heretics can avoid them and sneakily bind the sin, confront and eliminate them, swoop 
in to save them if they are in trouble, convince them to team up, etc.  
 
If they are caught and the Coven is able to report them, they immediately lose all reputation and all bonds they have are reduced to 1 strength. 
The third time this happens, they are publicly executed. 
 
Once a sin is successfully bound, the hunt is considered successful and the Coven that accomplished it cannot be reported or charged with 
infringement. After all, It’s every sinner for themselves. 
TOUGH LUCK 
As long as you’re stealthy enough, you can sway hunt bidding in your favour to begin with, especially when you’re bigger. You have a few options: 
●​
Vulture lower CAT hunts. Roll a skill of your choice, explaining how you force your way into the lower hunts as a higher-CAT heretic. For 
every point of difference between your CAT and the Hunt’s, gain +1D to the action. A heretic may expend a bond to add bonus dice equal 
to its strength, or spend a point of Reputation to automatically add 2 successes. On a success, you reduce the bidding cost by the number 
of successes. 
●​
Divide and conquer. Roll a skill of your choice, explaining how you strategize to scam the bid. A heretic may expend a bond to add bonus 
dice equal to its strength, or spend a point of Reputation to automatically add 2 successes. On a success, you split your group into up to 3 
separate “bidding groups” to dilute the pool, reducing the final bidding cost by the number of successes. 
●​
Take advantage of convenient misfortune. Roll a skill of your choice, explaining how you set up circumstances targeting one bidding 
group of your choice during the auction. A heretic may expend a bond to add 1 bonus die, or spend a point of Reputation to add 2. On a 
success, the group suffers a mishap whose severity is tied to the number of successes; the higher the worse the “misfortune”. This may 
cause the group to lower their bid, hesitate, or leave entirely. On a failure, the targeted group becomes aware of your interference. 
ASPHALT LAW 
In order to be respected in an area, you have to spend Reputation to cement yourself there. During downtime, you may spend any amount of 
Reputation and roll a skill of your choice to determine how you work the streets. The higher your Reputation there, the more you’re seen as the de 
facto top dogs. The Admin may make other NPC factions with their own Reputation levels based on their sphere of influence (a simple posse 
having 3 Rep, a crime boss having 6, and a mafioso having a terrifying 12 for example). The closer you approach the Reputation levels and turf of 
an existing faction, the higher tension will get. Unannounced non-diplomatic arrivals on land that is not your own can easily lead to tension even if 
you share a clan. 
 
When determining how an existing faction views you, the Admin can answer the following questions: 
●​
Is your level of Rep a threat to them? 
●​
Are you encroaching on their turf? 
●​
Are you new, or established allies? 
●​
Have you ever crossed them? 
●​
Do you already belong to them or their allies? 
 
If a faction’s view of nearby players is more hostile, they may find their movements and activities in the area to be subject to harsh scrutiny, with 
some more intense members engaging in active provocation. A player can choose to react however they want, but the higher the tension gets the 
higher the chance of things going south quickly. A good rule of thumb is to only pick a fight if you’re absolutely goddamn sure you can kill the boss 
of their boss. 
CASTLE IN THE SANDBOX 
U//H, like any system, is corrupt. Crime happens and runs unpunished all the time, everywhere. Plenty of heretics in high standing play with the 
rules like playdough as they see fit, and each part of the world has its own street laws and hierarchical power structure below the Round Table. 
Feel free to dig in and commit some crime if you want. Not like anybody’s gonna stop you if you’re on top. During downtime, as long as the players 
have a high enough Rep in the area and aren’t going against the grain of an existing faction, they may engage in some crimes of their choice. 
●​
Charitable protection services. You may roll a skill of your choice to determine how you get money out of a lower-Rep heretic or group 
of heretics in your area. This can either be entirely legit or a show of force. You may request any amount of Favours per downtime, but 
higher amounts may cause tensions to rise quicker. The choice is yours. But so are the consequences. 
○​
Fair (1-2 Favours), Pricy (3-4 Favours), Extortionate (5+ Favours). 
●​
Recreational item shops. You may roll a skill of your choice to salvage and sell items in a nearby black market to earn a relatively 
respectable amount of stolen cash. You may roll a skill of your choice to make and sell a mundane or supernatural consumable of your 
choice whose wealth CAT is equal to or lower than PSYCHE. 
●​
Asset management opportunities. You may roll a skill of your choice to get rid of a target, plain and simple. This can be an ambush, a 
kidnapping, a direct fight, or a silent assasination. As long as you are able to cover your tracks, any target whose CAT is equal to or lower 
than PSYCHE doesn’t need to be rolled for. Failure risks incurring the direct wrath of the Round Table Law, especially if your target is from 
another clan. 
 
 
 


 
THE BLACK MARKET 
 
Kit 
●​
Underhand Emblem: 6 Favours 
○​
A tell that you can flaunt 1/mission in a place of business, rolling a risk die: (1) the company is a CAIN shell company and the 
Countdown ticks up by 1, (2-3) the employee doesn’t know what you are doing, (4-5) the employee recognizes the symbol and 
gives you vague info or minor supplies before shooing you off, (6) the employee is another undercover binder who leads you to a 
storehouse, gain the benefits of a rest and restock all consumables spent so far. 
●​
White Mask: 3 Favours, 1 Rep 
○​
A mark of your membership to U//H. Comes in the color and pattern of your Clan, but you can design the eyes and horns how you 
wish. You look as if you are wearing civilian clothes to anyone not also wearing a mask. Stays on your face without a strap, and 
can be easily put on and removed. 
●​
Clan Brand: 4 Favours, 1 Rep 
○​
A mark of your membership to U//H. Comes in the logography of your Clan, but you can choose the complex parts of the design, 
where it is located, and how large it is. It appears as a mundane tattoo of your choice to anyone not with a matching tattoo. 
●​
Forged Bills: (Consumable) 2 Favours 
○​
Can easily pay transactions in the local currency up to 1,000 dollars a few times, or 10,000 once but very conspicuously. Bills are 
invalid and will not pass scrutiny by an actual bank but will fool anyone else. 
●​
Mechanic’s Kit: (Conspicuous) 4 Favours 
○​
Spend 1 KP to pull out one of the following: toolbox, (2KP) carjack, operation manual for a vehicle, industrial lubricant, spare 
mechanical parts, 30 ft of wire or extension cord, (2KP) small boat battery, shockproof gloves, welding helmet, power tool. 
●​
Criminal’s Kit: 4 Favours 
○​
Spend 1 KP to pull out one of the following: crowbar, glass cutter, pocket mirror, automatic lock pick, plantable bug (with small 
receiver), night camo bodysuit, heavy duty boltcutters, drugs, concealed weapon (can’t be spotted), car wiring kit 
●​
Engineer’s Kit: 4 Favours 
○​
Spend 1 KP to pull out one of the following: small laptop, bricking usb (consumable), large hard drive, handheld digital camera, 
spare electronic parts, wiring kit, wiretap, burnable CDs, handheld scanner (2 KP). 
 
Utility 
●​
Black Market Contact: (Consumable, 2 uses) 1 Rep 
○​
1/mission you can receive a specified drop off location and purchase any non-conspicuous item once. 
 
Residence 
●​
Apartment: 
○​
Costs 2 Favours/downtime for every 3 heretics. Comfortable, but sparse. Leases can be transferred to another site at a moment's 
notice. 
●​
Camping Supplies: (Consumable, 3 uses) 2 Favours 
○​
Expends one use per downtime. One heretic rolls a relevant skill, on a failure they roll a risk die. 
●​
Mobile Home: (Conspicuous) 12 Favours, 5 Rep 
○​
Spacious, if modest. Can either be a built-in vehicle or a trailer. Can comfortably fit up to 3 heretics, 5 if you count the driving 
seats. Getting it wrecked incurs a 5 favour fee to replace it. 
○​
All heretics gain 2 stress cap or 1 extra rest die. 
○​
Can store 2 free kits, which cost no KP to pull from. 
●​
Kitted Van: 8 Favours, 3 Rep 
○​
Inconspicuous looking windowless van on the outside. Houses a bunk bed and a small kitchenette. Can comfortably fit up to 3 
heretics, 5 of you don’t mind sharing bunks. Getting it wrecked incurs a 3 favour fee to replace it. 
○​
All heretics gain 2 stress cap. 
○​
Can store 1 free kit, which costs no KP to pull from. 
●​
Home Base: 35 Favours, 25 Rep 
○​
A full, medium-sized house. Warded with curses that make humans innately kinda ignore it. 
○​
Comes with 2 bedrooms, a bathroom, a large basement, 2 large rooms and 2 small rooms. 
○​
All heretics gain 1 injury cap, 2 stress cap, and 1 extra rest die. 
○​
Comes with 2 instances of “fully wrecked” replacement insurance before a 25 favour fee to get a new base.
 
 


 
ALL EQUALS UNDER HEAVEN 
 
The biggest part of any heretic’s identity is their clan. The ideology they subscribe to beyond the bounds of the CAIN dogma, the truth they aspire 
towards. It grants perks, pacts and restrictions on how a character can conduct themselves. Your clan is your everything, it’s what keeps you out 
of CAIN’s view, lets you bid for Hunts, and what finances you with its coffers.  
 
Power. Control. Purity. Peace. Prestige. Fortune. Truth. Freedom, clean and simple. 
 
Their tie to their clan is what grants them their contacts, their powers, and their standing. No heretic can be bound to more than one clan, and 
switching clans can be seen as traitorous unless in the most extraneous of circumstances, or via exchange programs for diplomacy. The premier 
exception to this dynamic are the Freelancers, also known as Mercs, Vultures, or Indies. They are the free and happy few who strike out from the 
social interplay of the grand Octagram, forging their own pacts and plays. Most Freelancers are simple guns for hire, but some are beginning to 
build their own gangs that can rival even one of the Octagon. 
DOG EAT DOG 
In recent years, following the banishment and call for execution of all Annihilation members and Jade themselves, there exists a gaping power 
vacuum as one of the eight seats of the Octagram now lies still and empty. Upstart clans and rival gangs are all stretching the boundaries of what 
is considered “acceptable” competition as they struggle for a seat in power. It’s a dark and tumultuous time for U//H. But then again, it always has 
been.  
 
Clans have become more and more isolationist, with burning bridges and failing diplomacies dotting the social landscape. Whether the heretics 
take advantage of or suffer in the face of these circumstances is entirely up to them. When starting off, relations between clans are incredibly 
rocky. It is highly unlikely a heretic coven will be comprised any more than 2 different clans, and it is very common for heretic covens to start off as 
Freelancers, gaining ties and bonds into various clans and working the crowd. 
 
Heretics should never start out with sufficient prestige or influence. You are the absolute scum at the bottom of the barrel, the lowest rung on the 
ladder. You will have to fight tooth, nail, and sin to claw your way up to comfort and security. You may not be actively suppressed like exorcists of 
CAIN, but you also lack any sort of guaranteed outside support or resources. Theft is common, even encouraged, and manipulation is your 
greatest tool. 
 
Your only goal is to survive to the next day.   
 
Welcome to the real world, hot shot. 
 
 


 
FREELANCER 
[BEHOLDEN TO NO ONE] 
 
It’s just you and me pal. You and me against the whole fucking planet. Let’s fucking do this. 
-​
Freelancer heretic gravestone quote 
LONE WOLF 
Undoubtedly the most popular option for a heretic, if they can manage it. Freelancers form a small but significant portion of the heretic population. 
They have the most variation in ideology, and often associate into rival Gangs that operate as pseudo-clans with no official power. Their actions 
cannot be scrutinized by the clans, so they are able to be more lax with their activities than the law of the Octagram permits. The only programs 
they have direct access to are the Black Market and Hunt Bidding. 
 
Freelancers are often larger than life, with eccentricities and weird quirks that bar them from entry into proper clans. They most often fit into the 
exaggerated Supervillain or Superhero archetypes, with their own individual wills and goals. They can be kind or genocidal, steadfast or fickle, or 
anything in between. Freedom, but at what cost? 
LIVE HARD, DIE HARDER 
Being a freelancer with no official standing or clan protection is living in constant threat. You have no recompense for being slighted by other clans, 
aside from exacting your own subtle revenge. You must constantly watch your back, defend your turf, and rely on community for support. You may 
not be bound to the dogma of a clan, but don’t think that makes you any more well off on your own. Allies are how you survive, how you raise your 
rank. 
 
How did you slip out of a faction? How are you managing to survive on your own? What connections do you have, and how do you play them? 
Who wants you dead, and how bad? It's tough being indie. 
 
Example Gangs: 
●​
The Hungry Dog Triad 
○​
A large and actively consuming gang spearheaded by three gerontocratic assassins. All sport incredibly vibrantly colored tattoos 
and thick spiked leather jackets.  
■​
Cheng Jianhong, management, triad law, and enforcement. 
■​
Du Jie, recruitment, combat, and invasions. 
■​
Gao Zhou, shipping, logistics, and black market. 
●​
Cutpurse Alley 
○​
You need something someone else has, you don’t call them. You drop a note down a sewer and 4 weeks later it's gone without a 
trace, tied up in a pretty bow in your favourite vault. Don’t think too hard about it. 
■​
Wickerman, a strange and silent figure who hands you your bill to pay. 
●​
No Man’s Band 
○​
Rag tag conglomerate of rogue mercs and hired guns with little in the way of an honor code or proper hierarchy. Somehow has 
access to massively restricted weaponry. 
■​
Bangin’ Marc, bear of a man with massive arm scars and a roguish charm. Always smoking. 
■​
Bonehead, shaved bald with a metal plate seared into place. Doesn’t talk, signs.
